# User-Flow Review — mywalnut

> Generated **2026-05-29** · branch `cleanup` · automated multi-agent review (261 agents, ~11.2M tokens, ~63 min).
> **169 findings**, each independently verified against the code by an adversarial pass that defaults to rejecting. Pipeline: map (Explore) → analyze across 4 lenses (conflicts · failure-paths · test-gaps · security) → verify → synthesize.

**This is an analysis artifact, not a changeset — no application code was modified.** Every item below is a *candidate*; confirm before acting. The severity and "real" verdicts come from an automated verifier and still deserve a human read — especially the security items, where a wrong call cuts both ways.

**Scope caveats:**
- **Tier A** flows (auth · conversation · checkpoint · resume · SMS · manual · settings) are the core user paths — weight these first.
- **Group text (Linq)** is deprecated and being sunset; per ADR-035 its webhook stays live permanently. Treat its findings as low priority — do not invest in removing it.
- **Voice** — confirm whether this flow is live or vestigial before acting on its findings.
- **test-gap** findings (81) dominate the low/medium tiers by design — you asked for the tests you need. The **security** (16) and **conflict** (27) findings are where the real risk concentrates.

## Cross-cutting themes

### 1. Service-role admin client bypasses RLS, but several routes never re-impose the userId scope it removes (IDOR class)
*Affects:* Jove conversation (in-app chat) · Manual view/edit · Admin (auth-scoping spot check)

createAdminClient() uses the service-role key and bypasses RLS, so route-level ownership checks are the ONLY boundary. /api/chat is missing it entirely: it reads conversations.mode by id alone (route.ts:64-71) and passes the client-supplied conversationId into callPersona, where loadConversationContext scopes the messages/conversations reads only by conversation_id (persona-pipeline.ts:104-117) — an authenticated attacker can read a victim's transcript into Jove's context AND write into the victim's conversation. Sibling routes (confirm/route.ts:138, session/summary/route.ts:39) get this right, proving it is an oversight, not policy. PATCH /api/manual/[id] relies entirely on a single .eq('user_id', user.id) clause with zero tests. The same class of unverified scoping recurs in the admin routes (conversations/messages/manual) whose 403 gate and audit-log admin_id sourcing are untested.

### 2. Mid-stream Anthropic stall has no client-side deadline — anthropicStream clears its only timeout the moment headers arrive
*Affects:* Jove conversation (in-app chat) · Checkpoint -> confirm -> entry · 1:1 text (SMS) inbound + outbound

anthropicStream sets a 60s AbortController but clears it at anthropic.ts:131 as soon as res.ok is confirmed, BEFORE the SSE body is read; parseAnthropicStream then awaits reader.read() in an unbounded loop. A 200-then-stall (network partition, truncated stream that never sends message_stop) hangs until Vercel's wall-clock kill across every Anthropic-streaming path. The AbortError handling in call-persona.ts only catches pre-header timeouts. Symptom is a frozen 'listening…'/forming indicator and burned function time on the in-app chat, the checkpoint-confirm follow-up, and (where guarded) the SMS post-confirm reply.

### 3. Anthropic-calling routes without rate limiting — the documented hard Security Rule is violated on three live entry points
*Affects:* Returning user / session resume · 1:1 text (SMS) inbound + outbound · Group text (Linq) [deprecated]

CLAUDE.md states 'All routes that call the Anthropic API must have rate limiting.' Three paths violate it literally: /api/conversations/complete calls generateSessionSummary (Haiku) with no limiter at all (sibling /api/session/summary gates the identical call); the SMS inbound path (webhook -> routeInboundMessage -> processTextMessage) is protected only by an in-memory Map that does not span Vercel instances and never reads the durable Postgres daily cap; and the group webhook reaches processGroupMessage with zero rate ceiling, made worse because mentionsPersona bypasses the gate's cooldown. All three are budget-drain vectors against an unbounded Anthropic spend.

### 4. Read-then-write / check-then-act with no DB-level uniqueness or lock — concurrency defeats idempotency and counters
*Affects:* Checkpoint -> confirm -> entry · Settings & account · Group text (Linq) [deprecated] · Jove conversation (in-app chat)

Multiple paths gate on an application-level read of state the DB also owns, with no FOR UPDATE lock, unique constraint, or atomic increment. The confirmed path is hardened (FOR UPDATE + partial unique index), but the parallel reject/refine/defer path is not, so a concurrent confirm-vs-refine overwrites a committed entry's status and contradicts the Manual. phone_numbers has no UNIQUE(user_id) so concurrent OTP sends duplicate-row or 500; verify can promote twice and double-send the greeting. The group conversation has no UNIQUE(linq_group_chat_id) so getOrCreateGroupConversation creates fragmented duplicates the catch-block re-query can never dedupe. The group messages_since_persona_spoke counter and the daily message cap are non-atomic read-then-write. Webhook event dedup (both Linq and the in-memory Maps) survives only within a warm instance.

### 5. Supabase query errors fail open/silent because the JS client returns {data,error} instead of throwing — routes return 200 with empty/degraded data, never reaching the catch
*Affects:* Manual view/edit · Jove conversation (in-app chat) · Returning user / session resume · Group text (Linq) [deprecated]

Several routes destructure only `data` and never check `error`, so a transient read failure looks identical to an empty result and the outer try/catch (which only fires on a thrown exception) never runs. GET /api/manual returns 200 {components:[]} on a query error → loadManual wipes the user's Manual with no error shown (reads as data loss). The anonymous checkpoint gate, the daily-limit count, and the group history load all silently fail open. generateSessionSummary clobbers a good stored summary with '' on a 200-but-empty Anthropic response. The returning-user resume path discards the messages query error and shows a blank conversation.

### 6. Client hooks hold copies of server-owned state with no reconciliation, and optimistic mutations are not rolled back on failure
*Affects:* Onboarding & auth · Settings & account · Manual view/edit · Checkpoint -> confirm -> entry · Admin (auth-scoping spot check)

MobileSettings/MobileLayout never unmount (display:none), so per-session refs (hasFetchedPhoneRef) and three independent persona_modes readers desync after a toggle or external change. MainApp's modalProgress is set once on mount and never refreshed, so modal 2 cannot follow modal 1 in one session. The optimistic confirm entry carries the message id (not the real manual_entries.id), so editing during Jove's follow-up stream PATCHes a non-existent row (404), and the EntryItem key-swap remounts the card mid-edit. Non-confirmed checkpoint actions optimistically flip status AND clear activeCheckpoint before the fetch, with no rollback on exhausted retry — DB stays pending, UI shows 'Discarded', no visible error. The admin user-profile fetch has no request-token guard, so out-of-order responses render the wrong user's data (and a misattributed audit-log row). addToBeta optimistically drops a waitlist row that the server may not have deleted.

### 7. Security-critical auth-scoping branches (403/404 ownership, OAuth allowlist gate, admin role check) are uniformly untested
*Affects:* Jove conversation (in-app chat) · Checkpoint -> confirm -> entry · Returning user / session resume · Settings & account · Manual view/edit · Waitlist & public landing · Onboarding & auth · Admin (auth-scoping spot check)

The exact branches that stop cross-user access have zero coverage and a regression would ship silently: /api/checkpoint/confirm 403 (conv.user_id !== user.id AND cross-conversation messageId), /api/session/summary 404 ownership, /api/conversations/complete 404 ownership, /api/account/delete auth scoping + 401, PATCH /api/manual/[id] user_id scoping, the entire /auth/callback OAuth allowlist-and-delete gate, middleware's /api/admin 403 role check, the verify-admin primitive, and the three admin data-viewing routes' 403 + audit admin_id sourcing. Tests always seed the matching owner, so the comparison never runs.

### 8. Partial-write side-effect paths (provider send, audit insert, post-confirm acknowledgement) mis-report or silently drop, inverting the user-facing result
*Affects:* 1:1 text (SMS) inbound + outbound · Settings & account · Jove conversation (in-app chat)

sendMessage runs the provider send and the messaging_events audit insert in the same try block, so an audit-insert throw after a successful delivery returns FAILED → OTP send reports a false 502 for a delivered code, and the SMS router logs a delivered Jove reply as failed. The post-checkpoint SMS path sends the literal 'Something went wrong on my end.' as Jove and persists it to history when Anthropic returns a non-text block. The acknowledgment-bubble insert and extraction_snapshot update discard their errors (no log), and the latter is fire-and-forget on the edge runtime without waitUntil. The crisis log writes a raw user_id where every other observability path hashes it.

### 9. PII (phone numbers, emails) leaks into logs in several places, diverging from an established hashing/redaction discipline
*Affects:* Settings & account · Group text (Linq) [deprecated] · Jove conversation (in-app chat) · Admin (auth-scoping spot check)

The codebase already hashes user ids in logEvent/recordApiError and the 1:1 router logs ids not phones, but the group path and a few routes drift: the OTP send route logs the full PostgREST error whose `details` embeds the raw E.164 phone on a unique violation; the group webhook logs raw added/removed handles and the nonPersonaHandles array; the Linq sender dumps the full response body (echoing outbound text) on every send; the admin beta-allowlist route logs raw email; and the reserved-keyword router logs raw inbound text. The crisis path logs raw user_id. None are catastrophic individually, but together they break the project's own log.ts PII contract.

### 10. Account-delete and dev-reset destructive cascades are non-transactional, error-swallowing, and contradict the messages↔manual_entries FK ordering
*Affects:* Settings & account

/api/account/delete runs six sequential deletes; only the final deleteUser is error-checked. manual_entries.source_message_id RESTRICTs deletion of messages, so deleting messages/conversations before manual_entries is a silent no-op that only completes via the profiles cascade — meaning a future 'add error checking' change would 500 every real user, and any change to the profiles cascade breaks deletion silently while returning {ok:true}. A mid-cascade failure can still delete the auth user, orphaning SET-NULL rows (linq_group_chats) the privacy policy says are gone. Both routes have zero tests. dev-reset (wired to a user-facing button) gets the ordering right and is the model.

### 11. The reconnect/recovery logic that justifies the Voice hook's complexity is dead due to a stale-closure capture, and the whole hook plus the raw-key token route are untested
*Affects:* Voice

connectWebSocket's ws.onclose closes over recordingState captured at WS-creation time (still 'idle' because setRecordingState('recording') runs later), so the mid-session reconnect guard at useVoiceInput.ts:134 never passes — the advertised 'one silent reconnect' is dead and a network blip silently loses everything the user says next. startRecording has no re-entrancy guard, so a second tap during the slow fetchTempKey orphans a live mic stream + WebSocket. There is no useVoiceInput.test.ts and no test for /api/voice/token, which hands the long-lived raw DEEPGRAM_API_KEY to the browser (mislabeled 'temp token') with no rate limit.

## Priority worklist

_Highest-impact fixes first. Each pairs with a test in the next section._

1. **[HIGH]** Add conversation-ownership check to /api/chat before callPersona (and defense-in-depth userId filter in loadConversationContext) — _Jove conversation (in-app chat)_ · security
   IDOR on the busiest write path: an authed attacker supplying a victim's conversationId reads the victim's transcript into Jove's context and writes/pollutes their conversation; service-role client bypasses RLS so the route check is the only boundary.
2. **[HIGH]** Re-run the OAuth beta allowlist check for ALL users (not just the 60s new-user window) and fail closed when deleteUser fails — _Waitlist & public landing_ · conflict
   One transient deleteUser failure plus a >60s retry permanently admits a non-allowlisted user — there is no downstream allowlist check anywhere, so the beta gate is silently defeated.
3. **[HIGH]** Drop the waitlist_anon_insert RLS policy and revoke INSERT from anon (service-role-write-only, via migration) — _Waitlist & public landing_ · security
   The anon key shipped to every browser can insert unbounded rows with multi-MB `source` directly, bypassing the route's rate limit, email validation, and length cap entirely.
4. **[HIGH]** Fix the mid-stream Anthropic stall: keep an idle/inactivity timeout on the body-read loop instead of clearing the timeout at headers — _Jove conversation (in-app chat)_ · failure-path
   A 200-then-stall hangs the function until Vercel's wall-clock kill with a frozen UI, across in-app chat, checkpoint-confirm follow-up, and SMS — the timeout class the code thinks it handles but does not.
5. **[HIGH]** Reactivate a pending checkpoint on session resume (initializeConversation must detect lastMsg pending and setActiveCheckpoint) — _Returning user / session resume_ · failure-path
   A user who closes the app mid-proposal returns to an inert, unactionable entry with no confirm/reject/refine affordance — a common-path dead end that loadConversation/switchConversation already handle but resume does not.
6. **[HIGH]** Stop the returning-text-user resume from restoring the synthetic 'text-channel' pseudo-conversation as real (filter is_text_channel before picking the restore target) — _Returning user / session resume_ · failure-path
   Any returning user who has texted Jove queries messages.conversation_id (uuid) with the literal 'text-channel' string, lands on a broken/empty session and triggers 'Failed to save message' — switchConversation special-cases this but initializeConversation does not.
7. **[HIGH]** Fix the multi-user group dead-end: set is_active=false for 2+ owner groups (or guard the prefetch Promise.all on null owner) — _Group text (Linq) [deprecated]_ · failure-path
   A multi-owner group stays active with null owner, so every later message throws in an unguarded Promise.all and is silently dropped forever while the gate counter drifts — Jove can never respond despite the intro promising it will.
8. **[MED]** Guard the concurrent confirm-vs-refine/reject race: add a status='pending' precondition to the non-confirmed UPDATE (or extend the FOR UPDATE RPC to those actions) — _Checkpoint -> confirm -> entry_ · conflict
   A confirmed write and a refined write that both pass the stale JS pending-check leave an orphaned manual_entries row whose source message reads 'refined' — the Manual tab and chat history permanently contradict, with contradictory signals fed to Jove's next prompt.
9. **[MED]** Add rate limiting to /api/conversations/complete (reuse sessionSummaryHour) and wire the durable Postgres daily cap into the SMS and group Anthropic paths — _1:1 text (SMS) inbound + outbound_ · security
   Three live routes call Anthropic with no durable rate limit, violating the literal hard Security Rule; complete/ is trivially exploitable for unbounded Haiku spend and the in-memory SMS/group Maps do not span Vercel instances.
10. **[MED]** Harden /api/account/delete: reorder manual_entries before messages, error-check every cascade step, fail closed on partial failure, and add tests — _Settings & account_ · failure-path
   Highest blast-radius route (irreversible delete) with zero tests; current ordering only works by accident via the profiles cascade, so it can return {ok:true} while orphaning user data — a direct privacy-promise violation.
11. **[MED]** Reconcile the optimistic confirm entry's id to the real manual_entries.id before it becomes editable (loadManual before the follow-up stream, or surface entry_id on the SSE event) — _Manual view/edit_ · conflict
   During the multi-second post-confirm stream the entry is editable with the message id as its id, so a real edit PATCHes a non-existent row (404) and the user's edit is silently lost in a window the product itself opens.
12. **[MED]** Make GET /api/manual check the query error and return 500 (don't 200 with empty components) — _Manual view/edit_ · failure-path
   A transient read error makes loadManual wipe a returning user's entire Manual to the empty-state invite with no error shown — reads as data loss on app load and post-confirm reconciliation.
13. **[MED]** Fix the Voice stale-closure reconnect: gate ws.onclose on an isRecordingRef, and add an isStartingRef re-entrancy guard to startRecording — _Voice_ · failure-path
   The advertised mid-session reconnect is dead code (onclose always reads 'idle'), so a network blip silently drops transcription; a double-tap during slow fetchTempKey orphans a hot mic + WebSocket streaming to Deepgram until unload.
14. **[MED]** Fix sendMessage so a successful provider send is never reported FAILED by an audit-insert throw (isolate the audit write from the return value) — _1:1 text (SMS) inbound + outbound_ · failure-path
   A delivered OTP code is reported to the user as a 502 (and a delivered Jove reply logged as failed) because the messaging_events insert sits inside the success try block — a bookkeeping failure inverts a real success.
15. **[MED]** Guard generateSessionSummary against overwriting a good summary with '' (only update on non-empty, TITLE-bearing text) — _Returning user / session resume_ · failure-path
   A single 200-but-empty Anthropic completion on the 30-min resume regeneration replaces last session's real summary with empty string, degrading returning-user continuity and blanking the conversation title.
16. **[MED]** Redact PII from logs: drop PostgREST `details` in the OTP route, raw handles in the group webhook, full response body in the Linq sender, and raw email in admin beta-allowlist — _Settings & account_ · security
   Several routine (non-error) log lines write raw phone numbers, emails, and echoed outbound text, breaking the project's own log.ts PII contract and the 'never log phone numbers' hard rule.
17. **[MED]** Guess-side OTP send: add a UNIQUE(user_id) + upsert (or 23505 retry) and send the SMS before persisting verified=false so a failed send can't destroy a prior verified link — _Settings & account_ · conflict
   No UNIQUE(user_id) means concurrent sends duplicate-row or 500 on a delivered code, and the existing-row branch overwrites a working verified link to verified=false before the send, so a send failure strands the user with no link and no code.
18. **[LOW]** Refresh MainApp.modalProgress after a modal-dismiss POST so modal 2 can follow modal 1 in one session — _Onboarding & auth_ · conflict
   Every email/Google signup hits this: the in-memory modalProgress never moves off its mount value, so the server-driven modal ladder only advances across page reloads.

Pin the UNIT: ts=floor(now/1000) passes, ts=now(ms) rejected as stale — freezes the contract so an edit can't silently flip it.

## Per-flow detail

### A · Onboarding & auth
12 findings — 1 high · 4 med · 7 low

<details><summary>Flow map</summary>

**Entry points:** `src/app/login/page.tsx`, `src/app/reset-password/page.tsx`, `src/middleware.ts`, `src/app/app/page.tsx`

**Steps:**
1. User lands on /login (public route) — `src/app/login/page.tsx`
2. User views EntryScreen (marketing/entry state) — `src/components/onboarding/OnboardingFlow.tsx`, `src/components/onboarding/EntryScreen.tsx`
3. User selects login/signup/forgot password modes in LoginScreen — `src/components/onboarding/LoginScreen.tsx`, `src/lib/supabase/client.ts`
4. Email/password signup with allowlist pre-check — `src/app/api/auth/signup/route.ts`, `src/lib/beta-allowlist.ts`, `src/lib/supabase/server.ts`, `src/lib/supabase/admin.ts`
5. Email confirmation and OAuth callback exchange — `src/app/auth/callback/route.ts`
6. Middleware auth gate and route protection — `src/middleware.ts`
7. User arrives at /app (authenticated) — `src/app/app/page.tsx`, `src/components/MainApp.tsx`
8. MainApp checks onboarding completion status on mount — `src/components/MainApp.tsx`, `src/app/api/onboarding-status/route.ts`
9. Post-login onboarding flow: InfoScreens → PersonaModeScreen → SeedScreen — `src/components/onboarding/PostLoginOnboarding.tsx`, `src/components/onboarding/InfoScreens.tsx`, `src/components/onboarding/PersonaModeScreen.tsx`, `src/components/onboarding/SeedScreen.tsx`
10. Anonymous users and auth conversion flow — `src/components/onboarding/SeedScreen.tsx`, `src/components/onboarding/AuthPromptModal.tsx`
11. Modal progression tracking for onboarding modals — `src/app/api/modal-progress/route.ts`, `src/components/MainApp.tsx`
12. Password reset flow — `src/app/reset-password/page.tsx`, `src/components/onboarding/LoginScreen.tsx`
13. Logout — `src/app/api/auth/logout/route.ts`, `src/components/MainApp.tsx`
14. Dev-only login bypass — `src/app/api/dev-login/route.ts`

**External deps:** @supabase/supabase-js (createClient, auth.signUp, auth.signInWithPassword, auth.signInWithOAuth, auth.signOut, auth.exchangeCodeForSession, auth.resetPasswordForEmail, auth.updateUser, auth.linkIdentity, auth.signInAnonymously, auth.getUser, auth.admin.deleteUser, auth.admin.listUsers, auth.admin.generateLink, admin table operations) · @supabase/ssr (createServerClient, createBrowserClient for SSR-aware session handling) · next/navigation (useRouter for client-side navigation) · next/headers (cookies() for server-side cookie access) · next/server (NextResponse, NextRequest for middleware and route handlers) · postgres (beta_allowlist table, profiles table with onboarding_completed_at and persona_modes columns, auth.users Supabase managed table)

**State touched:** auth.users (Supabase managed, created by signUp/signInWithOAuth, deleted if OAuth user not allowlisted) · profiles.onboarding_completed_at (set by SeedScreen on completion, read by /api/onboarding-status to gate PostLoginOnboarding) · profiles.persona_modes (set by PersonaModeScreen, default='general') · profiles.modal_progress (set by /api/modal-progress POST, read by GET to track onboarding modal state 0-3) · profiles.created_at (set by signup trigger, read by /api/modal-progress for signup_at_ms analytics) · beta_allowlist table (queried during signup and OAuth callback to gate access) · session cookies (set-cookie headers from /auth/callback via Supabase, chunked at 3180 chars, httpOnly, SameSite=Lax) · localStorage keys: mw_pending_conversion (set before Google OAuth conversion link), mw_first_session_completed, mw_signin_banner_dismissed (cleared by SeedScreen anonymous flow), mw_keys_migrated (migration marker), mw_last_manual_view (analytics for manual viewing)

**Existing tests:** `src/app/api/auth/signup/route.test.ts — comprehensive tests for POST /api/auth/signup including allowlist validation, email normalization, error handling, Supabase signUp integration (120 lines, vitest with mocks)`, `src/components/onboarding/onboarding-copy.test.ts — extensive regression tests for onboarding screen copy, validates presence/absence of specific text in EntryScreen, InfoScreens, SeedScreen, AuthPromptModal, MobileSession, etc. (287 lines, vitest file-content matching)`

</details>

#### Security (1)

- **[MED]** /api/auth/signup has no rate limiting — beta_allowlist is enumerable from an unauthenticated endpoint — `src/app/api/auth/signup/route.ts:12-50`
  POST /api/auth/signup is public, unauthenticated, and middleware does not run on it (matcher excludes api(?!/admin) in src/middleware.ts:97). It returns observably distinct responses based on beta_allowlist membership: an email not on the allowlist gets 403 {error:"not_allowlisted"} (route.ts:31-33), an allowlisted new email gets 200 {ok:true} (route.ts:50). With no rate limit, an attacker can POST email after email and enumerate exactly which addresses are on the private beta allowlist (and, via Supabase's signUp 'user already exists' message at route.ts:47, which allowlisted emails already have accounts). This is the same threat the codebase already defends against on its other public endpoint: /api/waitlist is IP-keyed rate-limited at 3/hour with the inline comment 'prevent enumeration' (waitlist/route.ts:26-31, rate-limit.ts:83-86), and the waitlist table is given no anon SELECT policy for the same reason. signup is the unguarded parallel. NOTE ON SEVERITY: this is a should-fix gap measured against the codebase's own established pattern, not a documented-rule violation — the written Security Rule mandates rate limiting only for 'routes that call the Anthropic API', and signup does not call Anthropic. Supabase's underlying auth endpoints (signInWithPassword, resetPasswordForEmail) carry their own server-side limits, but the custom allowlist pre-check in this route is exposed with none. I am confident the enumeration vector is real; whether allowlist membership is sensitive enough to prioritize is a product judgment for the founder.
  _→ Add an IP-keyed limiter mirroring the waitlist pattern: define e.g. `signupAttemptHour = makeLimiter(5, "1 h", "rl:signup")` in src/lib/rate-limit.ts, switch the handler to (request: NextRequest), and gate on `checkLimit(signupAttemptHour, getClientIp(request))` returning rateLimitedResponse() before the allowlist lookup — exactly as waitlist/route.ts:26-31 does. Consider also returning a uniform 200/202 'check your email' response for both the allowlisted and not-allowlisted cases (moving the waitlist redirect to a generic message) so the response shape stops leaking membership. Add a route test asserting the limiter is invoked, alongside the existing signup tests in route.test.ts._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified every checkable claim. The route at src/app/api/auth/signup/route.ts:12-51 has zero rate limiting (grep across src/app/api/auth/ confirms no limiter import), and middleware does not run on it (matcher at middleware.ts:97 excludes /api via the api(?!/admin) negative lookahead). The route returns 403 {error:"not_allowlisted"} for non-allowlisted emails (route.ts:31-33) versus 200 {ok:true} for allowlisted ones (route.ts:50), and this fork runs entirely in the route's own logic before any Supabase call (beta-allowlist.ts:24-41) — so an unauthenticated attacker can enumerate beta_allowlist membership at unlimited speed. The codebase's own /api/waitlist defends against exactly this on its parallel public endpoint (IP rate limit at waitlist/route.ts:26-31, plus no anon SELECT policy "to prevent enumeration" at waitlist/route.ts:62-64), so signup is the unguarded parallel. Severity is correctly medium: it's a should-fix gap against the codebase's established pattern, not a documented-rule violation (the Security Rule mandates rate limiting only for Anthropic-calling routes, and signup does not call Anthropic). One caveat the founder should note: the strong vector is the route-controlled 403-vs-200 allowlist oracle (unconditional); the secondary 'user already exists' sub-vector via the verbatim Supabase error at route.ts:44-47 only holds if Supabase email-enumeration protection is disabled, which is dashboard config not readable from the repo.</sub>

#### Conflicts (2)

- **[LOW]** MainApp's modalProgress goes stale after modal dismiss — modal 2 cannot follow modal 1 in the same session — `src/components/MainApp.tsx:129,397; src/components/mobile/MobileSession.tsx:785-803,116-129`
  modalState is set once on mount (MainApp.tsx:129) and never refreshed (setModalState has exactly one call site). The modal dismiss handlers POST to /api/modal-progress to advance the server's modal_progress (ChatWindowModal.tsx:56 target=1; PatternFormingModal.tsx:69 target=2), but the client copy MainApp passes down as modalProgress stays at its mount value. The modals form a server-driven ladder: modal1 shows when modalProgress<1, modal2 when modalProgress===1, modal3 auto-advance when modalProgress===2. Because the in-memory modalProgress never moves off its initial value within a session, a fresh real user who mounts at modalProgress=0 sees modal1 (0<1 true), dismisses it (server→1, local modal1Dismissed hides it), but modal2's gate modalProgress===1 is still evaluated against the stale 0 and is FALSE — so modal 2 never appears in that session. Same staleness defeats the modal3 auto-advance guard (MobileSession.tsx:118 modalProgress===2). The intended within-session progression only works across page reloads. Unlike the conversion finding this IS live: every email/Google signup is a non-anonymous user and hits these modals.
  _→ After a successful modal-progress POST, lift the new value back into MainApp's modalState (return the value via a callback the modal already has, or refetch /api/modal-progress on dismiss), so the next modal's gate sees the advanced progress. Add an integration test driving modal1 dismiss → assert modal2 becomes eligible in the same session._
  <sub>verified (high): The mechanical core is real and correctly located: setModalState has exactly one call site inside the mount-only effect (MainApp.tsx:129, []-deps), and no path re-fetches /api/modal-progress or updates modalState after a dismiss, so the local modalProgress passed down (MainApp.tsx:397) never advances within a session. A fresh signup mounts at 0, shows modal 1 (MobileSession.tsx:785, 0<1 true), dismisses it (server→1 via ChatWindowModal target=1, modal1Dismissed hides it locally), but modal 2's gate modalProgress===1 (MobileSession.tsx:796) is still evaluated against the stale 0 → false; same for the modal-3 auto-advance guard at :118. However, the finding's "modal 2 never appears in that session" and "every signup hits this" overstate it: modal 2 has four additional extraction gates (:799-802 hasLayerEmergingOrBeyond, concreteExamples>=1, non-empty emergingPatternSnippet) that the copy itself frames as "roughly halfway to your first entry," i.e. many turns deep — by which point the session has almost certainly remounted and re-read the advanced server value. Because the server POST is monotonic/idempotent (route.ts:102) and re-read on next mount, no progress is lost or corrupted. So the true effect is a one-load deferral of a sequential onboarding modal, not a broken flow and not data loss — a should-fix staleness bug, refined from medium to low. Caveat: the impact estimate rests on an inference about typical session length vs. extraction-gate timing; the staleness itself is certain.</sub>
- **[LOW]** SeedScreen's anonymous signInAnonymously branch is unreachable in the current wiring, contradicting the documented anonymous flow — `src/components/onboarding/SeedScreen.tsx:32-65; src/components/onboarding/PostLoginOnboarding.tsx:93`
  SeedScreen is now used only by PostLoginOnboarding (grep: that is the sole import/render site), which always passes onComplete and is itself only rendered when onboardingStatus==="needed" — a state reachable only for an already-authenticated user (the /api/onboarding-status fetch requires auth, MainApp.tsx:83). So handleSubmit's `if (user)` branch (line 32) always wins and the anonymous branch (lines 53-64: clears mw_first_session_completed + mw_signin_banner_dismissed, calls signInAnonymously(), router.push("/app")) can never run through normal navigation. This conflicts with the flow-map/system.md "Onboarding Flow" description of an entry→login→onboarding→seed anonymous path and with the whole isGuest/AuthPromptModal conversion subsystem in MainApp, which now has no producer of anonymous users. Engineering observation, not a runtime bug: the dead branch is harmless on its own, but it makes the two conflict findings above look inert when they are actually one wiring change away from live. Also note system.md Storage Keys still documents mywalnut_* keys while the code uses mw_* (MainApp.tsx:46-60 migration), a separate doc-drift.
  _→ Decide whether anonymous entry is still a product path. If retired, remove the anonymous branch + the isGuest/AuthPromptModal machinery (and update system.md). If retained, restore an entry point and treat the conversion-desync and callback-deletion findings as ship blockers for that path. Verify with the founder which it is — this is a product judgment, not an engineering call._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified the full wiring chain by direct reads. SeedScreen's sole render site is PostLoginOnboarding.tsx:93, which always passes onComplete; PostLoginOnboarding mounts only at MainApp.tsx:367 under onboardingStatus==="needed", reachable only for an authenticated user (middleware.ts:77-81 gates /app to logged-in users; page.tsx is force-dynamic so middleware always runs; /api/onboarding-status calls requireUser at route.ts:12). So SeedScreen.handleSubmit's getUser() always returns a user, the if(user) branch (line 32) always wins, and the anonymous branch (lines 53-64, signInAnonymously) is dead under normal navigation. Grep confirms SeedScreen.tsx:56 is the only signInAnonymously call in the repo, and OnboardingFlow.tsx now wires only entry→login (no seed view), so there is no producer of anonymous users left, leaving the isGuest/AuthPromptModal conversion subsystem (useChat.ts:465; MainApp.tsx:373,531) inert. This conflicts with docs/system.md:254-258 documenting the entry→login→onboarding→seed anonymous flow and guest-to-real conversion as live. The secondary storage-key drift is also confirmed: system.md:264-265 documents mywalnut_* keys while code uses mw_* (MainApp.tsx:46-60) and has no mywalnut_seed_text reference. This is correctly a doc/dead-code conflict, not a runtime bug — harmless on its own, no data/security impact — so severity stays low. The finding's framing about the two upstream findings could not be checked here, but the located SeedScreen and storage-key claims are accurate as written.</sub>

#### Failure paths (2)

- **[HIGH]** Guest email conversion calls onSuccess() before email is confirmed — under Supabase 'Confirm email' the conversion is incomplete but the UI reports success — `src/components/onboarding/AuthPromptModal.tsx:25-36`
  handleEmailSubmit calls supabase.auth.updateUser({ email, password }) and, on no error, immediately calls onSuccess(). With Supabase 'Confirm email' enabled (the default, and required for the email/password signup flow this app already uses — signup/route.ts passes emailRedirectTo), changing an anonymous user's email does NOT take effect immediately: Supabase sends a confirmation email to the new address and the email_change stays pending until the user clicks the link. updateUser returns with no error in that case, so the modal closes and the app proceeds as if the user is converted. Two concrete consequences: (1) The password IS set on the anonymous user but the email is unverified/pending, so the account the user thinks they created cannot be signed into by email until they confirm — and the modal gave them no instruction to check their inbox. (2) If the anonymous session is later GC'd by cleanup_stale_anonymous_users() (supabase/migrations/20260417000001) before confirmation, the manual is lost — exactly the outcome the modal ('Keep your manual / so you don't lose what you've built') promises to prevent. There is no 'check your email' UX anywhere in the conversion path (confirmed: no inbox/confirm/verify copy in AuthPromptModal or useChat). This is an engineering defect in how the conversion result is interpreted; whether to require email confirmation for guest upgrade is a product call, but reporting success on a pending email change is wrong either way.
  _→ Inspect the updateUser result. Supabase returns the updated user with a pending email change rather than a changed email; detect that (e.g. data.user.new_email set / email unchanged) and show a 'confirm your email to finish' state instead of calling onSuccess(). At minimum, add an integration test that mocks updateUser resolving with a pending-email-change shape and asserts the modal does NOT report success silently. Confirm the project's Supabase 'Confirm email' setting with a human — the correct branch depends on it, and this is the kind of auth-config-dependent behavior an agent cannot verify from the repo alone._
  <sub>verified (medium): The code claim is verbatim accurate: AuthPromptModal.tsx:30 calls onSuccess() the moment updateUser({email,password}) returns without error, and handleAuthSuccess (MainApp.tsx:216-220) only closes the modal — there is no session refresh, no is_anonymous re-check, and no "check your email" copy anywhere in the conversion path (grep confirmed; the only "check your inbox" string is LoginScreen.tsx:243 for password reset). The data-loss consequence is also concrete: cleanup_stale_anonymous_users() (migration 20260417000001:22-39) hard-deletes manual_entries/conversations/profiles/auth.users for any is_anonymous=true row older than 7 days, which contradicts the modal's "so you don't lose what you've built" promise. The whole defect is gated on one external fact I cannot read from the repo — whether Supabase "Confirm email" is enabled (no config.toml exists; it's a dashboard setting). The finding's inference that it is on is well-supported (signup/route.ts:6 comment says non-allowlisted emails "never receive a confirmation email" and line 41 passes emailRedirectTo, both of which presuppose confirmation), and under confirmation-on Supabase stages updateUser's email change as pending and resolves with no error while is_anonymous stays true — exactly the silent failure described. Confidence is medium, not high, only because that one setting can't be verified in-repo; the founder should confirm the Supabase Auth "Confirm email" toggle (and that converted-but-unconfirmed users retain is_anonymous=true) before treating the GC data-loss path as certain.</sub>
- **[LOW]** onboarding-status route comment claims 'client fails open' but both the route and the only caller fail closed — `src/app/api/onboarding-status/route.ts:5-6`
  The route header comment says 'The client fails open on error — a transient API failure must not lock a logged-in beta user out of the app.' The actual behavior is the opposite and is deliberate: the route returns 500 on query error (line 22-25), and the sole caller MainApp.tsx:79-104 explicitly fails CLOSED ('failing closed', sets onboardingStatus='needed' on non-ok and on fetch throw) with a correct rationale (don't let a transient hiccup bypass the age-gate/legal consent). The closed behavior is the right one for a compliance gate; the route comment is stale and contradicts it. Not a runtime failure-path bug — flagging because a future reader trusting the comment could 'fix' MainApp to fail open and silently punch a hole in the consent gate.
  _→ Update the comment at onboarding-status/route.ts:5-6 to state the client fails CLOSED on error (re-shows onboarding) so the compliance intent is recorded at the route, matching MainApp.tsx:70-104. No code change._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified directly: route.ts:5-6 comment says "The client fails open on error," but the route returns 500 on query error (lines 22-25) and the sole caller (confirmed via grep — MainApp.tsx is the only one) fails CLOSED, setting onboardingStatus="needed" on both !res.ok (line 90) and fetch throw (line 98), with its own comment (lines 70-78) correctly stating "Fail CLOSED" for the age-gate/legal-consent SeedScreen. The "needed" state routes to PostLoginOnboarding (line 365), confirming the consent gate. The comment is stale and directly contradicts the deliberate, correct fail-closed compliance behavior. This is a latent documentation trap, not a current runtime bug — behavior today is correct — so low severity is appropriate; the risk is a future reader "fixing" MainApp to match the comment and silently bypassing the consent gate.</sub>

#### Test gaps (7)

- **[MED]** Auth callback: zero tests for the OAuth allowlist gate and blocked-user deletion (security-critical) — `src/app/auth/callback/route.ts:74-115`
  GET /auth/callback is the ONLY gate on Google OAuth signups — email/password is pre-checked, but OAuth users are created during exchangeCodeForSession and only blocked here. There is no test file for this route (signup/route.test.ts matches the grep only via a comment string). Untested branches: (1) new OAuth user (created_at within NEW_USER_WINDOW_MS) NOT on allowlist -> admin.deleteUser called with user.id, redirect to /waitlist?reason=not_allowlisted, and all session cookies cleared (maxAge:0); (2) new OAuth user ON allowlist -> falls through to /app with session cookies intact, deleteUser NOT called; (3) returning user (ageMs >= NEW_USER_WINDOW_MS) -> allowlist NEVER checked, login unaffected; (4) the ageMs>=0 guard rejecting a future-dated created_at. A regression that flips the isNewUser comparison or skips the allowlist call would silently let non-allowlisted users into the beta with no test catching it.
  _→ Mock createServerClient.auth.exchangeCodeForSession, createAdminClient.auth.admin.deleteUser, and isEmailAllowlisted. Assert: blocked new user => deleteUser called once with user.id AND Location is /waitlist?reason=not_allowlisted AND set-cookie clears the session; allowed new user => deleteUser NOT called AND redirect to /app; user with created_at older than 60s => isEmailAllowlisted NOT called AND redirect to /app._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified: src/app/auth/callback/route.ts:74-115 contains exactly the described OAuth allowlist gate (isNewUser via ageMs>=0 && <NEW_USER_WINDOW_MS at line 77, isEmailAllowlisted at 80, admin.deleteUser at 87, /waitlist?reason=not_allowlisted at 96, cookie clear maxAge:0 at 104-111). This is genuinely the ONLY gate on Google OAuth signups — the email/password path (signup/route.ts:30-32) pre-checks the allowlist before signUp so no auth row is created, whereas OAuth creates the user inside exchangeCodeForSession (line 57); a grep confirms no other source file enforces the allowlist on the OAuth path. No test file exists for this route: find+grep for callback/route, app/auth/callback, and exchangeCodeForSession across all .test.* files returns nothing, and the lone signup/route.test.ts:103 match is the emailRedirectTo string in a signup-route test, not the callback. All four named branches are present and security-load-bearing. I refine high to medium: the code is correct as written and fails closed (beta-allowlist.ts:35-37 denies on lookup error), so this is an absent-regression-protection gap on a single-point-of-failure security gate (a Should-fix to track and close), not a present known-broken path (which would be a Blocker). One runtime caveat a human/test should confirm: whether a slow OAuth round-trip could push created_at past the 60s window and silently skip the gate.</sub>
- **[MED]** Middleware: no tests for the auth gate, admin block, or OAuth-code defensive redirect — `src/middleware.ts:39-83`
  middleware.ts has zero test coverage and gates every non-excluded route plus all /api/admin access. Untested security-critical branches: (1) /api/admin with user.app_metadata.role !== 'admin' (including user=null) -> 403 Forbidden JSON; (2) /api/admin with role==='admin' -> passes through; (3) unauthenticated user on a non-public route -> 302 to /login; (4) authenticated user on '/' or '/login' -> 302 to /app; (5) the public-route allowlist (lines 60-67) lets unauthenticated users through to /, /login, /reset-password, /privacy, /terms, /waitlist; (6) defensive '/'+?code -> rewrite to /auth/callback. The admin 403 branch is the access-control boundary for the entire admin surface and is unverified. A regression dropping the role check, or adding a path to isPublicRoute, would not be caught.
  _→ Mock createServerClient.auth.getUser. Assert per branch: getUser->null on '/api/admin/x' => status 403; getUser->{app_metadata:{role:'admin'}} on '/api/admin/x' => NextResponse.next (no 403); getUser->null on '/app' => redirect Location ends /login; getUser->user on '/login' => redirect to /app; getUser->null on '/privacy' => passes through; request to '/?code=abc' => redirect to /auth/callback preserving the query._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified: src/middleware.ts has zero test coverage (47 test files in repo, none reference middleware; vitest is the established harness) and lines 39-83 match the finding's described branches exactly. The test gap is genuinely real. However the finding overstates severity by calling the middleware admin block "the access-control boundary for the entire admin surface" — it is not. All 17 /api/admin routes independently call requireAdmin() (src/lib/admin/verify-admin.ts), which re-runs getUser() and re-checks app_metadata.role==='admin' server-side and returns its own 403, and requireAdmin is itself tested (waitlist/route.test.ts, beta-feedback/route.test.ts). So a regression dropping the middleware role check would NOT open the admin surface — the stated worst case is wrong. The untested non-admin branches (auth 302, logged-in redirect, isPublicRoute allowlist, OAuth ?code rewrite) are real and have no second layer, but defense-in-depth on admin plus a non-load-bearing OAuth guard make this a should-fix test gap, not high.</sub>
- **[MED]** modal-progress POST: idempotency, validation bounds, and 500 path untested — `src/app/api/modal-progress/route.ts:66-130`
  POST /api/modal-progress is documented as idempotent (target<=current is a no-op) and bounded to {1,2,3}, but has no test (the two modal-component test files matched the grep on the literal string only). Untested branches: (1) target<=current -> returns current value WITHOUT calling .update() (idempotency — the core contract); (2) target>current -> .update({modal_progress:target}) scoped by .eq('id', user.id) then returns updated value; (3) non-integer / target<1 / target>3 / missing body -> 400; (4) requireUser returns Response -> 401 short-circuit, no DB read; (5) readError or updateError thrown -> recordApiError called and 500.
