In src/app/api/checkpoint/confirm/route.test.ts the only three tests using action rejected/refined/deferred (lines 223-294) each set checkpoint_meta.status to a terminal value (rejected/refined/confirmed), so they hit the currentStatus !== "pending" early-return at route.ts:199 and never reach the write branch at lines 218-234. No test sends a non-confirmed action against a pending checkpoint, so the status map (deferred->"rejected", line 223), the increment-only-on-refined logic (lines 224-227), and the insertCheckpointActionMessage(admin, conversationId, action) call (line 234) are entirely unexercised; the mock at route.test.ts:30 also never inspects .update() args or the insertCheckpointActionMessage call. A swapped status map or misplaced +1 would pass every current test. The e2e file only covers the confirm_checkpoint_write RPC (the confirmed path), and call-persona.test.ts:102 / config.ts:76 only test the system-message string translation downstream of insertion, not the route's wiring. Medium, not high: the production code at 218-234 is correct today and the failure mode is a bounded behavioral/voice regression (POST-REJECTION line firing wrongly, or refinement_count drift) with no data loss or security exposure; not low because these are silent, behavior-defining branches with zero regression guard. Mechanical defect, not a design opinion.</sub>
- **[LOW]** No test covers the concurrent (interleaved) cross-action confirm race; existing coverage is sequential-only — `src/app/api/checkpoint/confirm/route.test.ts:206-267`
  The reject/refine/defer idempotency suite proves the route no-ops when it reads an already-terminal status (sequential ordering, including 'defer/reject after confirm' at :269). The confirm-checkpoint.e2e.test.ts:185 proves two concurrent 'confirmed' calls produce exactly one row. Neither covers two DIFFERENT actions racing (confirmed + refined / confirmed + rejected) where both pass the pending check before either commits — which is exactly the window the application-level status gate at route.ts:199 cannot close. This is the test that would have surfaced finding #1. Flagging separately because even before any fix lands, the absence of this test means the data-integrity gap is invisible to CI.
  _→ Add an e2e case (local Supabase, same harness as confirm-checkpoint.e2e.test.ts): seed one pending checkpoint, fire the confirm RPC and a simulated non-confirmed status update via Promise.all, then assert (a) exactly one terminal checkpoint_meta.status, (b) manual_entries row count is consistent with that status (1 if the surviving state is confirmed, 0 if not), and (c) no more than one checkpoint-action system message was inserted. Until finding #1 is fixed this test is expected to fail — that is the point._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified accurate. The route test (route.test.ts:206-294) only fires single POSTs against pre-set terminal statuses (sequential), and the only concurrent test anywhere (confirm-checkpoint.e2e.test.ts:185-219, Promise.all) fires two IDENTICAL 'confirmed' RPC calls. A grep for Promise.all across all *.test.ts returns only that one e2e file, so no test interleaves two different actions. The window is genuinely open: confirm goes through confirm_checkpoint_write with FOR UPDATE + a partial unique index (migration 20260417000003), but reject/refine/defer is gated only by an application-level status read at route.ts:198-199 on a row loaded earlier at :118-129 outside any lock, then writes a bare UPDATE at :229-232 with no lock or constraint — so confirmed+refined can both pass the pending check. It stays LOW because (a) it is a missing test, not a shipped defect; (b) the race needs a double-client/replay since both actions come from one overlay; and (c) even worst-case the confirmed path's RPC lock + unique index still prevents a duplicate entry, so the damage is a status/count inconsistency, not corruption. Caveat: I am reasoning statically, not from an executed interleaving; the correct home for the fix-validating test is the local-Supabase e2e file (a mocked route unit test cannot reproduce a DB race), and confirming the residual inconsistency is harmless under load warrants a real test.</sub>
- **[LOW]** confirmCheckpoint wrapper outer try/catch (thrown exception -> 'Something went wrong.') is untested — `src/lib/persona/confirm-checkpoint.ts:681-684`
  confirm-checkpoint.test.ts covers every RETURNED error (msgError, null meta, RPC error codes, no entry id) but never the case where the admin client THROWS — e.g. .single() or .rpc() rejects (connection drop mid-call). That path hits the outer catch at line 681 and returns `{ success: false, error: 'Something went wrong.' }`, which the route maps to a 500. grep confirms the string 'Something went wrong' appears in no wrapper test. The branch is simple, but it is the wrapper's only defense against an unexpected throw, and it produces a distinct user-facing message.
  _→ Add a test where mockChain.single (or .rpc) is made to reject (e.g. `mockChain.single = vi.fn(() => Promise.reject(new Error('boom')))`) and assert the result is `{ success: false, error: 'Something went wrong.' }` rather than an unhandled rejection._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified all three factual claims against source. The outer try at confirm-checkpoint.ts:577 wraps the entire body, so the catch at 681-684 is the sole defense against a thrown rejection from .single()/.rpc(), and it returns {success:false, error:'Something went wrong.'}. I read all 17 tests in confirm-checkpoint.test.ts: every one supplies a returned-error value (tableResponses/rpcResponse with an error field) or valid data via Promise.resolve — none makes the mock reject, so the catch is never exercised, and grep confirms 'Something went wrong.' appears in no test. The route (confirm/route.ts:159-167) does map this string to failWith(500,'rpc_fail'), since it isn't one of the two specially-cased messages. So the gap is genuine and the path is reachable (a true fetch-level abort/connection-reset throws rather than resolving to {error}). But severity is correctly LOW: the catch block is correct fail-closed behavior (no partial write, generic message, 500) with essentially no logic to get wrong — this is a coverage gap, not a defect. One nuance the finding slightly overstates: PostgREST resolves most DB errors as values (already tested via msgError/rpcError), so the throw path is narrower/rarer than 'connection drop mid-call' suggests, which only reinforces the low severity. This is a Consider, not a Should-fix.</sub>

### A · Returning user / session resume
14 findings — 3 high · 7 med · 4 low

<details><summary>Flow map</summary>

**Entry points:** `src/components/MainApp.tsx (useChat hook initialization)`, `useChat() hook - initializeConversation() [line 410]`, `/api/conversations GET route (load conversation list)`, `/api/chat POST route (send message to existing conversation)`

**Steps:**
1. 1. Client detects returning user — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`
2. 2a. Load conversation list — `src/app/api/conversations/route.ts`
3. 2b. Determine session resume intent — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`
4. 3a. Load conversation messages — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`
5. 3b. Check session freshness and trigger summary — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`
6. 4. Load session summary and context — `src/app/api/session/summary/route.ts`
7. 5. Generate summary via Anthropic — `src/lib/persona/generate-summary.ts`
8. 6. Client prepares session state — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`
9. 7. Client renders session UI — `src/components/MainApp.tsx`, `src/components/mobile/MobileSession.tsx`
10. 8a. User sends message to existing conversation — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`, `src/app/api/chat/route.ts`
11. 8b. Load conversation context for LLM prompt — `src/lib/persona/call-persona.ts`, `src/lib/persona/persona-pipeline.ts`
12. 8c. Build system prompt with returning-user context — `src/lib/persona/system-prompt.ts`
13. 9. Stream LLM response — `src/lib/persona/call-persona.ts`, `src/lib/anthropic.ts`
14. 10. Parse and emit SSE events — `src/lib/anthropic-sse.ts`, `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`
15. 11. Client appends assistant message — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`
16. 12. Mark conversation complete on session end — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`, `src/app/api/conversations/complete/route.ts`

**External deps:** Anthropic API (claude-3-5-haiku-20241022 for summaries, claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022 for conversation) · Supabase (PostgreSQL): tables conversations, messages, manual_entries, profiles · Upstash Redis: rate limiting (chatAuthMinute, chatAuthDay, sessionSummaryHour) · Vercel Functions (waitUntil for background jobs) · Sendblue (SMS; webhook at /api/webhooks/sendblue/route.ts) · Linq (group chat; webhook at /api/linq/webhook/route.ts)

**Existing tests:** `src/lib/hooks/useChat.test.ts: buildChatMessageFromEvent (checkpoint marshaling)`, `src/lib/persona/generate-summary.test.ts: buildTranscript (transcript formatting)`, `src/lib/persona/persona-pipeline.test.ts: buildPromptOptionsFromContext, validateMaterialQuality, applyCheckpointGates`, `src/lib/persona/call-persona.test.ts: mapSystemMessages, applySlidingWindow, shouldEmitUploadOpener, stripCheckpointFromText, findRetryStormDuplicate`, `src/db-contract.e2e.test.ts: DB schema validation for messages, conversations, manual_entries (drift canary)`

**Notes:** RETURNING USER / SESSION RESUME FLOW: When a returning user navigates back to mywalnut, the initializeConversation() hook [useChat.ts:410] checks if conversations exist. If yes, it restores the most-recent conversation [line 492], loads its message history from the DB [lines 499-517], and optionally regenerates the session summary if the last message is older than 30 minutes [lines 534-541]. The summary lives in conversations.summary column and is generated via Anthropic Haiku to create a context block for Jove on the next message turn. When the user sends a message to the existing conversation, callPersona loads full ConversationContext [persona-pipeline.ts:90] which includes the prior summary, all manual entries, and extraction state. These are embedded in the system prompt so Jove can reference the user's prior work and continue coherently. The sessionOrigin state var tracks whether the session was 'new', 'explore', or 'existing' [useChat.ts:141, 491]. When a conversation is marked complete, generateSessionSummary is called if missing [conversations/complete/route.ts:37]. Rate limiting is handled by Upstash Redis at session-summary (10/hour) and chat endpoints. All DB writes are atomic; checkpoint state is persisted to checkpoint_meta JSON column on messages. The flow is designed to preserve context across sessions without re-querying the full conversation history on every turn (sliding window applied at load time [persona-pipeline.ts:152])."

</details>

#### Security (1)

- **[MED]** /api/conversations/complete calls the Anthropic API with no rate limiting (violates a hard Security Rule) — `src/app/api/conversations/complete/route.ts:36-38`
  The Security Rule in CLAUDE.md states plainly: "All routes that call the Anthropic API must have rate limiting." This route does not. When conv.summary is null it calls generateSessionSummary(conversationId, admin) (route line 37), which issues an Anthropic Haiku call at src/lib/persona/generate-summary.ts:41 (anthropicFetch with SUMMARY_MODEL). The route imports no limiter — there is no checkLimit/checkLimits anywhere in the file, unlike its sibling /api/session/summary which gates the identical Anthropic call with checkLimit(sessionSummaryHour, user.id) (summary/route.ts:21). Auth and ownership ARE enforced (requireUser at line 8, conv.user_id !== user.id check at line 25), so this is not cross-user access — the exposure is Anthropic budget. An authenticated user can POST { conversationId } directly for any of their own conversations whose summary column is null and trigger an unbounded series of Sonnet/Haiku token spends. Each successful call writes the summary (generate-summary.ts:51-54), so a single conversation self-heals after one hit; but a user with N summary-less conversations (or one whose summary write transiently fails and stays null) has no per-route ceiling, and the flow's own client fires this fire-and-forget on every 'New session' click (useChat.ts:1106) and on the second caller at useChat.ts:1136. This is the same budget-drain class the codebase already defends against everywhere else (see rate-limit.ts module-load hard-fail in prod, lines 34-39, written specifically so 'bot traffic or runaway clients can[not] drain our Anthropic budget'). I am confident this is a real omission against the documented rule; whether the realistic blast radius warrants a blocker is a judgment call given ownership is enforced and summaries are idempotent per-conversation.
  _→ Add the same gate /api/session/summary already uses. There is a fitting limiter to reuse — sessionSummaryHour (rate-limit.ts:68, 10/hour) — or define a dedicated one. Insert after auth: `const limit = await checkLimit(sessionSummaryHour, user.id); if (!limit.success) return rateLimitedResponse(limit);` and import { sessionSummaryHour, checkLimit, rateLimitedResponse } from '@/lib/rate-limit'. Add a test asserting a 429 once the limiter is exhausted, mocking the Anthropic call so no real tokens are consumed (per the Tests hard rule)._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed in code. /api/conversations/complete/route.ts imports no limiter (only requireUser, createAdminClient, generateSessionSummary) and at line 36-37 calls generateSessionSummary when conv.summary is null, which issues a real Anthropic call at generate-summary.ts:41. This violates the unconditional Security Rule in CLAUDE.md:104 ("All routes that call the Anthropic API must have rate limiting"). The sibling /api/session/summary gates the identical call with checkLimit(sessionSummaryHour, user.id) (summary/route.ts:21), so the established pattern is missing here and auth+ownership alone (which both routes share) is the codebase's own evidence that ownership is not considered sufficient. Two corrections to the finding: the model is Haiku only (SUMMARY_MODEL = claude-haiku-4-5, config.ts:40), not Sonnet/Haiku; and empty conversations cost nothing because generate-summary.ts:34-36 returns null before any Anthropic call. Blast radius is genuinely bounded (authed, owner-scoped, idempotent self-heal after one successful write at generate-summary.ts:51-54), so medium is right — a documented-discipline violation but not data loss or a broken user path. Fix is one checkLimit call mirroring the sibling route. The mechanical omission is high-confidence; whether the per-user Haiku spend warrants beta-blocking is a budget judgment the founder owns.</sub>

#### Conflicts (1)

- **[LOW]** sessionOrigin client state can desync from the restored conversation's actual server state (status='completed') — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts:490-495 and src/app/api/conversations/route.ts:25-30`
  initializeConversation sets sessionOrigin='existing' and restores allConversations[0] for ANY most-recent conversation, including ones whose server status is 'completed' (the /api/conversations query at route.ts:25-30 does not filter on status, and startNewSession/complete set status='completed' at conversations/complete/route.ts:30-33). So a returning user whose last act was ending a session (status completed, summary written) is dropped back INTO that completed conversation as if active, and the next sendMessage posts to it — there is no server-side guard that rejects writes to a completed conversation (the chat route only checks ownership/mode, route.ts:64-71). The 'completed' status becomes advisory only; the client's 'existing' origin and the server's 'completed' status disagree about whether the conversation is live. Low severity because appending to a completed conversation is benign today (no code branches on status at write time), but it is a latent contradiction: any future logic that trusts status='completed' to mean 'no further messages' would be violated by this resume path. Confidence: high on the mechanics; whether reopening a completed conversation is the intended UX is a product call.
  _→ Decide the contract: either exclude status='completed' from the restore candidate in initializeConversation (mirroring intent that completing a session starts fresh next time), or explicitly flip status back to 'active' on resume so the column stays truthful. Document whichever is chosen in system.md so the 'completed' status has a single defined meaning._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed real and currently present. /api/conversations/route.ts:25-30 returns conversations ordered by updated_at with no status filter, and useChat.ts:490-495 restores allConversations[0] with sessionOrigin='existing' unconditionally — even restoring latest.summary, which is only written for completed conversations (complete/route.ts:36-37). Completed status is produced by startNewSession (useChat.ts:1106) and startExploration (useChat.ts:1136) via complete/route.ts:30-33, so a returning user whose last act ended a session can be dropped back into that completed conversation as if live. I verified exhaustively that no server write path gates on conversation status: the chat route reads only `mode` (route.ts:67), persona-pipeline.ts:115 selects extraction_state/summary/mode, and every `.eq("status","active")` is on phone_numbers/linq tables while every checkpoint_meta.status is the messages-row field — none read conversations.status. So the client 'existing'/server 'completed' contradiction is real, but severity is correctly low/latent: appending to a completed conversation is benign today because nothing branches on the field (schema-map/page.tsx:155 documents it as a marker, not a terminal write-lock). Note the existing mw_new_session guard (useChat.ts:484) only suppresses the same-tab post-New-session refresh and does NOT cover the returning-user/new-tab case, so the path is genuinely reachable. Whether reopening a completed conversation is the intended UX is a product call, not settled by code.</sub>

#### Failure paths (5)

- **[HIGH]** Returning user with any text-channel message restores the synthetic "text-channel" pseudo-conversation as a real conversation, querying a uuid column with a non-uuid string — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts:492-527`
  initializeConversation restores allConversations[0] unconditionally (line 492). The /api/conversations route forces the synthetic text-channel entry to index 0 via result.unshift(...) whenever the user has ANY text-channel message (route.ts:138), independent of updated_at ordering. So for any returning user who has texted Jove (a primary entry point per CLAUDE.md: Sendblue 1:1 text), allConversations[0].id === "text-channel". The restore path then runs supabase.from("messages").select().eq("conversation_id", "text-channel") (lines 499-503). messages.conversation_id is `uuid NOT NULL` (squash_baseline.sql:271), so Postgres rejects the literal "text-channel" with an invalid-uuid-syntax error; the destructured { data: dbMessages } comes back null. With dbMessages null, the code falls to nonSystemMessages.length === 0 (lines 523-524) and calls triggerPersonaOpener("text-channel"), which POSTs /api/chat with conversationId: "text-channel". On the server convId is truthy so no new conversation is created (route.ts:136-137); callPersona then inserts a user/assistant message with conversation_id "text-channel" (call-persona.ts:440-449, 743-751), hitting the same uuid error and emitting "Failed to save message" / "lost the thread." Net effect: a returning text user can land on a broken/empty session instead of their real most-recent conversation. switchConversation (line 967) explicitly special-cases "text-channel"; initializeConversation does not — this is the asymmetry. Confidence: high on the wrong-conversation selection and the uuid mismatch (column type and unshift are both confirmed in code). Medium on the exact downstream symptom, because Supabase's edge behavior on an invalid-uuid filter (hard error vs. empty set) should be confirmed against the running DB rather than inferred.
  _→ In initializeConversation, skip synthetic entries when picking the conversation to restore: const latest = allConversations.find(c => !c.is_text_channel); guard the whole restore block on latest being defined, and fall through to the brand-new-user branch otherwise. Add a useChat init test where /api/conversations returns [{id:'text-channel',is_text_channel:true,...}, {id:'<uuid>',...}] and assert conversationId resolves to the uuid, not 'text-channel'. This is the same filter switchConversation already applies at line 975._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed against code. /api/conversations unshifts the synthetic {id:"text-channel"} entry to index 0 for ANY user with a text message, ignoring updated_at (route.ts:138); initializeConversation then restores allConversations[0] with no text-channel special-case and runs supabase.from("messages").eq("conversation_id","text-channel") (useChat.ts:492-503) against a uuid NOT NULL column (squash_baseline.sql:271). PostgREST casts the literal to uuid, which fails (22P02), so supabase-js returns data:null deterministically (a parse-time cast failure, not an edge case), driving dbMessages null -> nonSystemMessages [] -> triggerPersonaOpener("text-channel"), which POSTs conversationId:"text-channel"; the chat route treats it as truthy (route.ts:136), skips conversation creation, and callPersona's assistant insert hits the same uuid error (call-persona.ts:743). switchConversation special-cases "text-channel" (useChat.ts:967) but initializeConversation does not — the asymmetry is real. Net: any returning user whose entry point was text (a primary documented channel) lands on a broken empty session on open, and MobileSession.tsx:778 disables the composer for text-channel, blocking in-place recovery. Two minor prose errors in the finding (the failing insert for the null-message opener is the assistant insert at 743, not the user insert at 440-449 which is gated by message!==null; and the error copy is "Failed to save message. Try again." not "lost the thread") do not change the conclusion. Could not execute against a live DB (no local Supabase running), but the invalid-uuid behavior is stable Postgres semantics, so confidence is high.</sub>
- **[HIGH]** Resume never reactivates a pending checkpoint — user lands on a dead, unactionable proposal — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts:490-543`
  The session-resume path (initializeConversation, restoring the most-recent conversation) loads message history but never inspects the last message for a pending checkpoint and never calls setActiveCheckpoint. Compare loadConversation (useChat.ts:1071-1084) and switchConversation, which both explicitly detect lastMsg.checkpoint_meta?.status === 'pending' and call setActiveCheckpoint. MobileSession gates the interactive trigger card on activeCheckpoint (src/components/mobile/MobileSession.tsx:433-436: isPendingCheckpoint requires activeCheckpoint && activeCheckpoint.messageId === msg.id). On resume activeCheckpoint is null, so isPendingCheckpoint is false and the message falls through to the 'historical Plate' branch (MobileSession.tsx:608-656). For status 'pending' the status badge is suppressed (line 622 only renders when status !== 'pending') and there is no 'Tap to review' affordance. Concrete consequence: a user who closes the app while Jove has an open checkpoint proposal, then returns, sees the composed entry text rendered as an inert bubble with no way to confirm, reject, or refine it. The only escape is to send a new message, after which the pending checkpoint is still never resurfaced as actionable. This is a real user-facing dead-end on a common path (returning mid-proposal). Confidence: high — verified by tracing the resume branch and the MobileSession render switch.
  _→ In initializeConversation's restore branch, after setMessages, replicate loadConversation's detection: read the last non-system dbMessage and, if is_checkpoint && checkpoint_meta?.status === 'pending', call setActiveCheckpoint with {messageId, layer, name: composed_name || name, content, composedContent}. Add a useChat test that initializes with a restored conversation whose final message is a pending checkpoint and asserts activeCheckpoint is set. Consider extracting the pending-checkpoint detection into one shared helper so resume, switchConversation, and loadConversation cannot drift again._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed by tracing the real resume path. initializeConversation (useChat.ts:490-543) loads messages and calls setMessages but never inspects lastMsg.checkpoint_meta?.status for 'pending' and never calls setActiveCheckpoint — unlike loadConversation (1071-1084), which does. With activeCheckpoint null, MobileSession's interactive trigger card is gated off (lines 433-436 require activeCheckpoint.messageId === msg.id), so a pending checkpoint message falls into the historical Plate branch (608-656) where line 622 suppresses the status badge for 'pending' and line 620 renders the composed entry as an inert bubble with no confirm/reject/refine affordance. The finding is correct on the dead-end and actually understates the gap: switchConversation (956-1037), the sidebar/SessionDrawer 'onSelectSession' path a real user hits to re-open a past session, ALSO lacks pending detection (its only setActiveCheckpoint is the reset to null at 963). loadConversation — the one path with the detection — is wired only to handleSimulationEvent and the desktop dev-tools bridge (MainApp.tsx:252), i.e. simulation tooling, not normal resume. So both production paths to a pending-checkpoint message leave it unactionable. One precision note on the claim's tail: sendMessage (563+) doesn't resurface it either, but I did not fully trace whether the backend re-proposes on the next turn; the client-side dead-end for the existing proposal is conclusively real regardless. Severity high is fair — it's a recoverable-only-by-starting-over dead-end on a common path (closing the app mid-proposal), not data loss, and not a documented-discipline breach, but it strands a confirmation-gated entry, which is core to the product.</sub>
- **[MED]** generateSessionSummary overwrites a valid stored summary with empty string on a successful-but-empty Anthropic response — `src/lib/persona/generate-summary.ts:49-54`
  After the Haiku call, summary = extractResponseText(response) and the code unconditionally writes it: admin.from('conversations').update({ summary }). extractResponseText (src/lib/anthropic.ts:41-43) returns '' when content[0].type is not 'text'. anthropicFetch only throws on non-2xx or a missing content[0]; a 200 whose first block is a non-text type (e.g. a refusal/tool block) yields '' and is NOT caught by the try/catch at line 57. The 30-minute resume trigger (useChat.ts:534-540) regenerates the summary for an existing conversation that already has a good summary, so a single empty/degenerate completion replaces last session's real summary with an empty string. That summary is then injected into Jove's next-turn context (loadConversationContext reads conversations.summary at persona-pipeline.ts:114-117 and 202), degrading returning-user continuity, and surfaces as a blank conversation title in the list (extractTitle returns null on empty). Confidence: high on the mechanism; medium on real-world frequency (empty-but-200 Anthropic responses are rare but do occur).
  _→ Guard the write: only update conversations.summary when the extracted text is non-empty (and ideally contains the required 'TITLE:' line). On empty, return the existing summary unchanged rather than clobbering it. Add a generate-summary test mocking anthropicFetch to return a content block with type !== 'text' (or empty text) and assert the existing summary is preserved (no update issued)._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed real and present. extractResponseText (src/lib/anthropic.ts:41-43) returns '' for a 200 whose content[0].type !== "text"; anthropicFetch only throws on non-2xx or missing content[0], so this case is not caught. generateSessionSummary (src/lib/persona/generate-summary.ts:49-54) writes summary unconditionally inside the try, persisting '' . The 30-minute resume trigger (src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts:534-540) fires POST /api/session/summary, and that route (session/summary/route.ts:43) calls generateSessionSummary with NO if-missing guard (unlike conversations/complete/route.ts:36 which guards), so an existing good summary is regenerated and can be overwritten with ''. The empty summary is then dropped from Jove's prompt (system-prompt.ts:806 guard is falsy on '') degrading returning-user continuity, and extractTitle (conversations/route.ts:6-10) returns null, blanking the title. Medium, not blocker: no user-content loss, the field self-heals on the next good completion, and the trigger (empty-but-200 response) is rare; but it is an unguarded failure path on the exact resume flow. One minor inaccuracy in the finding: a refusal returns a text block, so the realistic trigger is a non-text first block (e.g. tool_use), not a refusal — does not change the verdict.</sub>
- **[LOW]** /api/conversations/complete has no try/catch and parses the body unguarded — malformed/missing input throws a raw 500 with no error capture — `src/app/api/conversations/complete/route.ts:12`
  const { conversationId } = (await request.json()) as {...} runs with no surrounding try/catch and no field validation. Unlike /api/session/summary (route.ts:13-59) and /api/chat (route.ts:23-203), this route has no outer try/catch and never calls recordApiError. A malformed JSON body (or a client that posts no body) makes request.json() reject, producing an unhandled rejection that Next surfaces as a bare 500 — invisible to the error dashboard. If conversationId is undefined, the subsequent .eq('id', undefined).single() errors and returns 404 (safe-ish), but the parse-throw case is the real gap. This route is on the session-end path (startNewSession at useChat.ts:1106 and startExploration at 1136 both POST here fire-and-forget), so a flake here silently skips the conversation-complete + summary-on-missing generation (route.ts:36-38), meaning the just-ended session may never get its summary and never get marked completed. Confidence: high — the route source has no outer try/catch.
  _→ Wrap the handler body in try/catch mirroring the summary route, call recordApiError in the catch, and validate conversationId is a non-empty string before the DB calls (return 400 on missing). Because callers fire-and-forget and ignore the response, also confirm the failure is at least logged so a stuck-active conversation with no summary is diagnosable._
  <sub>verified (high): The structural claim is accurate: src/app/api/conversations/complete/route.ts:7-41 has no outer try/catch and never calls recordApiError, unlike its siblings session/summary (route.ts:49-58) and chat (route.ts:24-25), so a request.json() reject becomes an unhandled rejection / bare 500 invisible to the dashboard. But the finding's stated consequence is overstated: both call sites (useChat.ts:1106 and :1136) build the body with JSON.stringify({ conversationId }) and set Content-Type, so the real session-end path can never produce a malformed/empty body — the parse-throw case is only reachable by a hand-crafted request. With a valid conversationId the route completes normally and summary still generates (generate-summary.ts has its own internal try/catch returning null on failure), so no real session silently loses its summary. This is a genuine but low-impact consistency/hardening gap, not a medium failure on the resume flow; refined to low. (Separately and out of scope: this route calls generateSessionSummary, an Anthropic call, with no rate limiting unlike summary/route.ts:21-24 — a real Security-Rules deviation worth its own finding.)</sub>
- **[LOW]** Resume summary regeneration is fire-and-forget client-side; a failed/half-applied write is only console-logged, with no user signal and no retry — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts:534-541`
  On resume, if the last message is older than 30 minutes the client POSTs /api/session/summary fire-and-forget and only .catch-logs failures. This is an intentional non-blocking design and is acceptable for the current turn (the in-flight message still uses the previously stored summary, read at persona-pipeline.ts:114-117). The minor failure-path note: if the summary route 500s (rate limit at session/summary route.ts:21-24 returns 429, or Anthropic fails), the stale/empty summary persists silently and there is no retry on the next resume unless the 30-minute condition happens to hold again. Combined with finding #2 (empty overwrite), a single bad regeneration can leave a returning user with a permanently empty summary until a brand-new session generates one. Confidence: high on behavior; low severity because summary is non-critical to the current turn and self-heals on the next conversation.
  _→ No blocker. If finding #2 is fixed (don't overwrite with empty), this self-mitigates. Optionally surface nothing to the user but ensure the summary route's 429/5xx path doesn't leave a degraded summary (covered by #2's non-empty guard)._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed in code. At useChat.ts:534-541 the resume regen is exactly as described: gated only on >30min since last message, fired fire-and-forget, with failures handled solely by .catch(console.error) — no user signal, no retry outside the time gate. The in-flight turn reads the stored summary at persona-pipeline.ts:114-117 and never blocks on the fetch, so the non-blocking design is intentional and correct. The low-severity note is genuine: a 429 (session/summary route.ts:21-24) or Anthropic failure leaves the summary unrefreshed with no retry until the 30-min condition recurs. One caveat that softens, not negates, the finding: the 'permanently empty summary' framing (its tie to finding #2) overstates the real path. The common failures — timeout, 429, 5xx, empty content — all make generateSessionSummary return null WITHOUT executing the update (generate-summary.ts:57 catch; the update at :51-54 is skipped), so the prior summary is preserved, not emptied. Only a narrow case (Anthropic returns 200 with a non-text first content block, so extractResponseText returns "" at anthropic.ts:42) actually writes an empty string. That shape is unlikely for a plain summarization call. So the behavior is real and currently present, severity is correctly self-rated low (summary is non-critical to the current turn and self-heals on the next >30min regen), but the empty-overwrite blast radius is smaller than the finding implies.</sub>

#### Test gaps (7)

- **[HIGH]** Session-summary route 404 ownership-scoping branch is untested (security-critical) — `src/app/api/session/summary/route.ts:33-41`
  The ownership check (conv.user_id !== user.id → 404) is the only thing stopping user A from triggering an Anthropic summary call against user B's conversation by id. The existing route test (src/app/api/session/summary/route.test.ts) hard-codes the admin mock to always return { id: "c1", user_id: "u1" } and authenticates as u1 (lines 14-16, 46-48), so the ownership comparison ALWAYS passes — the 404 branch never runs. The only branches covered are the 429 rate-limit and the 200 happy path. A regression that dropped or inverted the `conv.user_id !== user.id` guard (e.g. scoping the query by id alone, which it already does at line 36) would pass the entire existing suite. This is exactly the auth-scoping path the lens flags as priority.
  _→ Extend src/app/api/session/summary/route.test.ts: add a case where the admin .single() resolves { id: "c1", user_id: "someone-else" } while getUser returns id "u1", and assert res.status === 404 and that generateSessionSummary was NOT called. Add a second case where .single() resolves { data: null } (conversation not found) and assert 404. Also assert the 401 branch: getUser returns { user: null } → res.status === 401 (requireUser path)._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified against the code, the test, and a live test run. route.ts:33-37 queries conversations by id alone via the service-role admin client (RLS bypassed), so the only cross-user protection is the guard at route.ts:39 (conv.user_id !== user.id -> 404). The lone test file hard-codes the admin mock to return user_id "u1" for every call (route.test.ts:14-15) and authenticates as u1 (:46-47), so the comparison is always false; only the 429 (:60-62) and 200 (:72-73) branches run. A regression dropping or inverting that guard passes the whole suite. The path is genuinely security-critical: past the guard, generate-summary.ts:41-54 makes an Anthropic call on and writes a summary back to the target conversation, so a broken guard is a cross-user read AND write on a no-RLS query — high severity for the gap is justified. Caveat: this is a missing test, not a present vulnerability; the shipped guard is correct today.</sub>
- **[MED]** No test covers the resume path's message-load failure or pending-checkpoint restoration — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.test.ts:1`
  Existing useChat tests cover only buildChatMessageFromEvent (checkpoint marshaling). There is no test for initializeConversation's restore branch: (a) the Supabase messages query returning an error so data is undefined — the code at useChat.ts:505 guards with `if (dbMessages)` and silently leaves messages empty, showing the returning user a blank conversation with no error (a query failure is indistinguishable from an empty conversation); and (b) restoring a conversation whose final message is a pending checkpoint (finding #1). The destructured `const { data: dbMessages }` at line 499 also discards the query error entirely, so an RLS/connection failure here produces a silent empty render rather than a retry or error state.

(Scope) useChat.test.ts (109 lines) tests only buildChatMessageFromEvent; initializeConversation/loadConversation are not exported and have zero coverage. (b) is stronger than a test gap: it exposes a live behavioral defect. Severity medium, not high: no documented coverage discipline is violated (system.md:279 treats tests as expected, not mandated per-branch), no data loss or security hole; the defect degrades a real resume case but the pending checkpoint persists in the DB and is recoverable. Its regex /^TITLE:\s*(.+)/ is coupled across files to the exact prompt format generateSessionSummary emits (generate-summary.ts:45 prompts "TITLE: [title]\n\n"), yet generate-summary.test.ts covers only buildTranscript — nothing pins the prompt-to-parser contract, so a prompt drift (lowercase 'Title:', leading blank line) would make extractTitle silently return null and drop every conversation's title with no failing test. The synthetic text-channel entry (route.ts:133-149: 80-char truncation + unshift) is real and is consumed by switchConversation's id==='text-channel' special-case (useChat.ts:967); it's also unshifted to index 0, so the initial resume at useChat.ts:490-496 (latest = allConversations[0]) frequently targets the synthetic entry itself, making the synthesis more load-bearing than the finding states, not less. useChat.test.ts exists but only tests the pure buildChatMessageFromEvent helper, not the resume or text-channel paths. Medium is correct: working today, silent-regression risk on a future prompt edit, no data-loss or security impact — a should-fix, not a blocker.</sub>
- **[LOW]** conversations GET partial-failure resilience (count/preview sub-query errors) untested — `src/app/api/conversations/route.ts:68-89`
  The route deliberately logs but does not fail when the message-count or first-user-message sub-queries error (lines 68-73): countResult.error and userMsgResult.error are logged, then the code proceeds to build countMap/previewMap from whatever data exists (lines 75-89). This is a reasonable resilience choice — a count failure should not blank the whole conversation list during resume — but it is an undocumented, untested failure path. If a refactor made countResult.data null while leaving the guard, message_count would fall back to 0 for every conversation; nothing asserts the route still returns 200 with the conversation rows intact rather than throwing. The outer try/catch (lines 152-162) only covers the primary conversations query and unexpected throws.
  _→ Add a mocked-admin route test where the count sub-query resolves { data: null, error: <err> } but the primary conversations query succeeds: assert res.status === 200, the conversations array is still returned with the correct ids, and message_count is 0 for each (graceful degradation). Optional companion: previewMap fallback to null when the user-message query errors. Low severity — confirms intended resilience rather than guarding a known bug._
  <sub>verified (high): The code reading is accurate and the narrow factual claim holds: src/app/api/conversations/route.ts lines 68-73 log sub-query errors without returning, lines 75-89 build countMap/previewMap behind truthiness guards (if countResult.data / if userMsgResult.data), the route still returns 200 with conversation rows via the || 0 / || null fallbacks, and no test file exists for this route (none in the directory, no .test.ts references it). So the resilience path is genuinely untested. But the finding overstates it: this is a test gap, not a bug. The route is correct as written, and the finding's own hypothetical (a refactor leaving countResult.data null) is already handled by the existing guard plus the || 0 fallback, so no latent defect is present. Only 11 of 42 API routes have adjacent tests and no documented discipline requires failure-path coverage, so this is a low-value Consider, not a tracked issue.</sub>

#### Failure paths (5)

- **[MED]** Inbound dedup and per-user SMS rate limiting rely on serverless instance state the server does not actually own across instances — duplicate Jove invocations and bypassed rate limits under concurrency — `src/lib/linq/message-router.ts:69-93, src/app/api/webhooks/sendblue/route.ts:298-308`
  Two distinct claims in the flow map ('Unique partial index... catches Sendblue retries', step 4; '20 msgs/5min per user', step 8) lean on state that does not behave as a single source of truth under Vercel's concurrent-instance model. (1) The 23505 DB index (migration 20260417000005:38-40) IS a real cross-instance guard for dedup — that part is sound. BUT the insert-then-check ordering at route.ts:283-308 means TWO concurrent Sendblue retries (Sendblue retries 3x within a 45s window per the header comment) can BOTH pass the insert attempt before either commits: Postgres serializes the unique index so one gets 23505, but if the first insert has not yet COMMITTED when the second SELECT-less insert fires, the loser correctly short-circuits — this is fine for the DB. The real gap is (2): isUserRateLimited (router.ts:82-93) and unknownNumberCooldown (router.ts:69) are module-scope in-memory Maps. The comment at router.ts:69 and 77 honestly says 'serverless warm instance only', but the flow map presents '20 msgs/5min per user' as an enforced guardrail. Under Vercel, N concurrent warm instances each hold an independent counter, so the effective ceiling is 20*N and a burst spread across cold-started instances bypasses it entirely. For an LLM-cost guardrail on a path that bypasses the Upstash-protected /api/chat route (router.ts:73-77 says exactly this), the only real defense-in-depth against runaway Anthropic spend is per-instance and therefore not a real ceiling. This is the SMS path's sole rate limit and it does not hold under the concurrency it exists to guard against.
  _→ Move the per-user SMS rate limit to the same Upstash limiter used by /api/chat (keyed by userId), so the ceiling is global. If Upstash is intentionally optional (ADR-038), back it with the Postgres daily-message-limit that ADR-038's reversal references ('Postgres-backed daily message limit', SHA dbd433b) — that store IS cross-instance. Document explicitly that the in-memory Maps are best-effort only and not the real ceiling. No test can catch this without a multi-instance harness; flag for a real load test._
  <sub>verified (high): Real and currently present. The SMS inbound path's only per-user rate guard is `isUserRateLimited` (message-router.ts:82-93), backed by the module-scope `userMessageCounts = new Map(...)` at line 78. I confirmed the SMS call chain (message-router.ts -> persona-bridge.ts -> persona-pipeline.ts) imports neither `@/lib/usage` (the Postgres daily cap, imported ONLY by src/app/api/chat/route.ts) nor `@/lib/rate-limit` (Upstash, imported only by HTTP routes under src/app/api). So under Vercel's nodejs runtime (route declares runtime='nodejs', no vercel.json, no concurrency pinning) each warm instance holds an independent counter: effective ceiling ~20*N and cold-started bursts bypass it. The code's own comment (lines 72-80) confirms this is intentionally the SMS path's sole defense-in-depth against Anthropic spend on a path that bypasses /api/chat, and ADR-039 lists per-number inbound rate limits as future work requiring Upstash. The finding correctly concedes the 23505 DB dedup index is sound (cross-instance correct) and isolates the rate limiter as the live gap. Refined to medium, not high: the Anthropic call is gated behind a verified-phone lookup (message-router.ts:160-178, verified=true required), so blast radius requires a verified account rather than an open endpoint. Note for the founder: the finding understates one point in his favor's opposite direction — the Postgres daily cap does NOT backstop this path at all (it never runs on SMS), so the SMS path has no durable per-user ceiling whatsoever. This is a mechanical enforcement-topology defect, not a design opinion about the 20/5min value. High confidence on the mechanism; the only thing requiring a real test rather than code reading is the production concurrency multiplier (how many instances actually spin up), which should be confirmed against Vercel metrics or a burst test before sizing a fix.</sub>
- **[MED]** Successful SMS send + audit-insert throw is mis-reported as FAILED, causing a false 502 on OTP send (delivered code reported as not sent) — `src/lib/messaging/send.ts:94-108 (catch at 147-159); consumed at src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:167-177`
  In sendMessage(), the provider call (sendMessageViaSendblue, line 89) runs BEFORE the audit-row insert (line 94), and both sit inside the same try block. If the provider send succeeds (the SMS is actually delivered) but the messaging_events insert then throws — a transient Supabase connection drop, pool exhaustion, or query error, which are exactly the partial-write conditions this lens targets — control jumps to the catch at line 147. The catch writes a SECOND audit row with status:'FAILED' and returns { status: 'FAILED' }, even though the message was delivered. Downstream consequence in the OTP send route (route.ts:167): a successfully-delivered verification code is reported to the user as a 502 'Failed to send code.' The hash is already stored, the code is live for 10 minutes, but the UI tells the user it failed. The user re-requests (resetting otp_attempts to 0 per route.ts:128), while the first delivered code remains valid. Same false-negative hits the router at message-router.ts:296, where a delivered Jove reply logs 'message_send_failed'. The root cause is that the audit write is not isolated from the success-return path — a bookkeeping failure is allowed to invert the user-facing result of an action that already succeeded.
  _→ Move the audit insert outside the success/failure decision: capture the provider result first, return status from the provider result, and perform the audit insert as a best-effort step whose failure is logged (console.error) but never changes the returned status (mirror the .then(({error}) => ...) pattern used in persona-pipeline.ts:485 and message-router.ts:192). At minimum, wrap each audit insert in its own try/catch so an insert throw cannot reach the outer catch. There is currently NO test file for send.ts (src/lib/messaging/*.test.ts does not exist) and the phone-route tests fully mock sendMessage (route.test.ts:88), so this path is entirely unexercised — add a unit test: provider send resolves OK, admin.insert rejects, assert returned status stays the provider's status (not FAILED)._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed in code. In sendMessage() (src/lib/messaging/send.ts), sendMessageViaSendblue() at line 89 performs the actual HTTP POST that delivers the SMS and returns before the audit insert at lines 94-108, with both inside one try block. The insert is a bare `await admin.from("messaging_events").insert(...)` with no error handling. A transport-level failure (connection reset, pool/socket exhaustion, DNS, timeout) rejects the underlying fetch promise, which `await` re-throws into the catch at 147-159; that catch returns { status: 'FAILED' }. The consumer at phone/route.ts:167-177 then returns a 502 "Failed to send code" even though the code was delivered and the hash is live for 10 minutes (verify/route.ts confirms the code is valid), and the user's re-request resets otp_attempts to 0 (route.ts:129) while the first code stays valid. The same false negative logs 'message_send_failed' at message-router.ts:303-310. Two scope caveats that lower severity from high to medium: (1) a query-level Supabase error (constraint/column) returns { error } rather than throwing, and since the insert result is never checked, that common class is silently swallowed and does NOT hit the catch — only genuine transport rejections invert the result, narrowing the trigger to true partial-write/connection conditions; (2) MESSAGING_PROVIDER currently routes 1:1 to Linq in production per ADR-035, and the Linq branch (lines 120-146) computes status from linqResult.ok and only throws on the same transport class, so the defect is real on both branches but its blast radius depends on the active provider. Real bug, behavior-preserving fix is to move the audit insert out of the success path or wrap it in its own try/catch so a bookkeeping failure cannot invert a delivered send; not a blocker because the trigger is a transient infra fault, not a routine path.</sub>
- **[MED]** Post-checkpoint follow-up: if Anthropic returns an unexpected shape (not a throw), the user is sent the literal fallback string 'Something went wrong on my end.' as if it were Jove — `src/lib/linq/persona-bridge.ts:91-92 and 95-108`
  processTextMessage builds fullText as `response.content?.[0]?.text || 'Something went wrong on my end.'` (line 91). anthropicFetch already throws on unexpected shapes (anthropic.ts:95), so in the normal case this fallback never triggers. But the optional chaining means that if content[0] exists with a non-text first block (e.g. a tool_use or empty-text block that slips past anthropicFetch's `!json?.content?.[0]` guard, which only checks existence, not type), `.text` is undefined and the literal 'Something went wrong on my end.' is used. For the post-checkpoint path (messageText===null, line 95-101), this fallback string is then INSERTED into messages as role:'assistant' AND sent to the user via sendMessage at message-router.ts:438 with contentKind:'jove'. The user just confirmed an entry to their Manual and receives 'Something went wrong on my end.' as Jove's acknowledgement, and that error string is persisted into their conversation history where Jove will see it on the next turn. The post-checkpoint path has no try/catch around the shape (the catch at message-router.ts:444 only fires on a thrown error, not on this soft fallback), so the bad acknowledgement is committed silently.
  _→ Guard the post-checkpoint path explicitly: if response.content?.[0]?.type !== 'text' or text is empty, do not persist or send the conversational fallback as a Jove turn — for the post-checkpoint case, fall back to the system-message confirmation ('Written to manual.') the catch block already uses (message-router.ts:448), and skip inserting the error string into history. Add a test: post-checkpoint call returns content:[{type:'text',text:''}], assert the persisted/sent message is not 'Something went wrong on my end.'_
  <sub>verified (medium): The defect is real and currently present. At persona-bridge.ts:91-92 `fullText = response.content?.[0]?.text || "Something went wrong on my end."`; the anthropicFetch guard at anthropic.ts:73 checks only that content[0] EXISTS, not that content[0].text is a non-empty string, so an empty-text completion (content:[{type:"text",text:""}], e.g. a refusal or zero-token max_tokens stop) returns falsy and triggers the literal fallback without throwing. On the post-checkpoint path (messageText===null, lines 95-101) that string is persisted as role:"assistant" and returned, then sent to the user as Jove (contentKind:"jove") at message-router.ts:442; the try/catch at message-router.ts:444 only fires on a throw, so the bad acknowledgement is committed silently after the user confirms an entry. I am correcting the finding's specific mechanism: the cited tool_use example is impossible here because AnthropicRequest (anthropic.ts:15-21) has no tools field and the persona call sends none, so the API will not emit a tool_use block — the realistic trigger is an empty-text block, not a non-text block. Severity medium is right: it requires an uncommon empty/zero-length response, degrades one message and pollutes conversation history, but is not data loss/security/discipline-breaking; the same pattern is duplicated at group-bridge.ts:242. Confidence is medium, not high, because whether this model/config actually emits an empty-text block at runtime is behavior I cannot verify by reading code — there is no persona-bridge.test.ts, and a stub returning text:"" would confirm it deterministically.</sub>
- **[MED]** SMS inbound Anthropic path has no durable rate limit — diverges from the 'all Anthropic routes must be rate-limited' invariant — `src/lib/linq/message-router.ts:78-93, src/lib/linq/persona-bridge.ts:83`
  The Security Rule in CLAUDE.md states 'All routes that call the Anthropic API must have rate limiting.' The SMS inbound webhook (/api/webhooks/sendblue) reaches Anthropic via routeInboundMessage -> processTextMessage -> anthropicFetch (persona-bridge.ts:83). Its ONLY rate guard is the module-level in-memory Map `userMessageCounts` (message-router.ts:78, USER_RATE_MAX=20/5min). On Vercel serverless this Map starts empty on every cold start and is not shared across concurrent warm instances, so it does not durably bound spend — it is best-effort defense-in-depth, not a real limit. The durable Postgres limiter that ADR-038's 2026-05-19 reversal cites as the thing that 'covers chat' (checkDailyMessageLimit in src/lib/usage.ts) is invoked ONLY in src/app/api/chat/route.ts:121 — the web path. The SMS path never calls it. Net: a verified user (or, per ADR-039, an attacker who has the forgeable sb-signing-secret and a known verified phone number) can drive unbounded Anthropic calls through the SMS path across cold-started isolates with no durable ceiling. Note this is an asymmetric gap: SMS user-turns DO get inserted as role='user' (persona-bridge.ts:56) and thus count toward the web /api/chat daily cap, but the SMS path itself never reads that cap, so the protection only runs one direction. This is a documented, knowingly-accepted divergence (ADR-038 reversal de-scoped Upstash; the daily cap was assumed sufficient) rather than an unknown bug — but as written it does not satisfy the literal security invariant for the SMS entry point. CONFIDENCE: high on the mechanical gap (the durable limiter is demonstrably not called on this path); the severity is medium not high because the blast radius is token spend on a single verified/known phone, not data exposure, and the 6-digit phone-ownership requirement plus Sendblue's own delivery limits bound trivial abuse.
  _→ Call checkDailyMessageLimit(admin, userId) in routeInboundMessage (message-router.ts) right after the verified phone lookup at line 174 and before processTextMessage, returning RATE_LIMIT_MSG when not allowed — this reuses the existing Postgres-backed durable cap with zero new vendor and closes the asymmetry. Either implement that, or amend the Security Rule to explicitly carve out the SMS path and name the in-memory Map + per-phone Sendblue limits as the accepted control. This is a founder decision (product/risk call on beta abuse tolerance), so present it as a recommendation; given ADR-038's history I'd recommend wiring the durable cap rather than weakening the rule._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified all mechanical claims. The SMS inbound path (sendblue/route.ts:363 and linq/webhook/route.ts:596 -> routeInboundMessage -> processTextMessage -> anthropicFetch at persona-bridge.ts:83) is guarded ONLY by the module-level in-memory Map userMessageCounts (message-router.ts:78, 20/5min), which the code's own comments label "serverless warm instance only" — it resets on cold start and is not shared across warm isolates, so it is not a durable spend ceiling. grep confirms the durable Postgres limiter checkDailyMessageLimit has exactly one non-test caller, src/app/api/chat/route.ts:121, and is never imported or called on the SMS path; the sendblue route has no checkLimit/Upstash call. The asymmetry claim holds: SMS turns insert role:"user" (persona-bridge.ts:56) into a user-owned conversation and the cap's count query (usage.ts:64-72) has no channel filter, so SMS turns inflate the web cap while the SMS path never reads it. Severity is medium not high: blast radius is token spend on a single verified/known phone (no data exposure), and the ADR-039 attacker variant additionally needs the forgeable shared secret plus a known verified phone. Two refinements to the finding: (1) it slightly overstates the "literal invariant" breach — a rate limiter does exist on the route, so the rule's letter ("must have rate limiting") is met; what is lacking is durability, a gap against the spirit, which the finding itself concedes. (2) The most useful thing it surfaces is that ADR-038's 2026-05-19 reversal claims chat token abuse is "covered" by the daily cap "with chat covered" — but that coverage is true only for /api/chat, not for the SMS entry point that reaches the identical Anthropic call, so the ADR's reasoning has a documented hole here. This is a real, currently-present should-fix/track item, not a blocker, and closing it is gated on the same Upstash provisioning that ADR-038/039 already defer. Confirming durable behavior under concurrency would require Upstash live or a real load test — confident on the static gap.</sub>
- **[LOW]** Checkpoint confirmed but post-confirmation Jove call killed by timeout leaves the user with no acknowledgement and a silent success — `src/lib/linq/message-router.ts:421-462 (confirm branch)`
  On a YES reply, confirmCheckpoint() writes the entry to the Manual (line 423), THEN processTextMessage(userId,null,conv.id) is awaited (line 433) to generate the spoken acknowledgement, THEN sendMessage delivers it (line 438). The entry write and the acknowledgement send are two separate awaited steps with no atomic relationship. The try/catch at line 444 correctly handles a THROWN error from processTextMessage by sending 'Written to manual.' as a fallback. But this whole branch runs inside the same webhook function that has no maxDuration (finding above): the entry is already committed to manual_entries, and if the second Anthropic call (line 433) is slow and the platform kills the function before line 438 sends, the user gets total silence after replying YES. From the user's side the confirmation appears to have failed, but the entry IS in their Manual. A re-send of YES would hit handleCheckpointResponse again, find the checkpoint's status already 'confirmed' (not 'pending', so line 419 returns null), and fall through to a normal Jove turn — so they cannot re-trigger the acknowledgement. This is the partial-write 'entry saved but follow-up missing' case the lens calls out, made reachable by the missing function-duration guard.
  _→ Same maxDuration fix as the webhook timeout finding mitigates the platform-kill window. Independently, consider sending a terse synchronous confirmation ('Written to manual.') immediately after confirmCheckpoint succeeds (before the second Anthropic call), so a kill during acknowledgement generation still leaves the user with a delivered confirmation. Verify behavior under a slow post-confirm Anthropic response; this is the same Vercel-timeout class that warrants a real test rather than inference._
  <sub>verified (high): Every causal link is confirmed in code. message-router.ts:423 awaits confirmCheckpoint (a committed single-transaction RPC) BEFORE the separate awaited processTextMessage (Anthropic call, persona-bridge.ts:83) at line 433 and sendMessage at 438 — non-atomic, ordering as claimed. The sendblue webhook awaits routeInboundMessage inline (route.ts:363) with no maxDuration (grep NONE) and no vercel.json override, so a platform kill in the gap between the committed entry write and the SMS send is reachable. The confirm_checkpoint_write RPC flips checkpoint_meta.status to 'confirmed' (migration line 113), which is the exact field the router gate at line 419 reads (meta.status !== 'pending' → null), so a re-sent YES falls through to a normal Jove turn (line 533) and cannot re-trigger the acknowledgement — the dead-end is real. The try/catch at 444 only catches in-process throws, not a wall-clock function kill, so it does not cover this case (the finding states this correctly). I refine severity DOWN from medium to low: the entry is correctly persisted (no data loss or corruption — this is a missing SMS acknowledgement / trust gap, not an integrity failure), the window is narrow (kill must land between a committed RPC and an SMS send after the Anthropic call burned the budget), and the entry is still visible in-app. Real and worth tracking as a should-fix, not a blocker. Caveat: whether the timeout actually fires depends on the deployed nodejs maxDuration default and real p99 latency, which I verified is unguarded in code but did not measure in production.</sub>

- **[LOW]** Outbound status callback: unknown/garbage status value (statusRank null) branch untested — `src/app/api/webhooks/sendblue/route.ts:140-147`
  The webhook outbound tests cover QUEUED/SENT/DELIVERED/FAILED, the unknown-handle path, and out-of-order/idempotent no-ops. They do not cover an unknown status string on a known row: statusRank returns null at :94, the handler logs outbound_status_unknown_value and returns without updating :140-147. A regression that treated an unrecognized status as rank 0 (or wrote it) would corrupt a row's status and no test would flag it.
  _→ Add an outbound case: existing row {status:'SENT'}, payload.status='BOGUS', assert mockUpdates is empty and that console.warn fired with 'outbound_status_unknown_value'. Confirms unknown statuses are dropped, not persisted._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified directly. The branch exists and is correct: statusRank returns null for any non-{QUEUED,SENT,DELIVERED,FAILED} value (route.ts:84-96), and handleOutboundStatusUpdate logs outbound_status_unknown_value and returns without updating when incomingRank===null (route.ts:140-147). The test gap is real: route.test.ts is the only suite hitting POST, and all seven outbound tests use status SENT/DELIVERED/FAILED (all recognized) — none sends a garbage status against a known row. grep confirms outbound_status_unknown_value appears only in the source, never in any test. So the unknown-value-on-known-row branch has zero coverage and a regression there would go uncaught — the test-gap claim is accurate and correctly located, hence isReal=true at low severity. Caveat: the finding's stated corruption mechanism is partly wrong. If the guard were removed, incomingRank would be null, and the next check `null <= currentRank` coerces to `0 <= currentRank`, which is true for every real row (rank >= 0) — so the regression would hit the idempotent no-op at :149, not write the garbage status. A bad write would only occur on a row whose own status is already unrecognized (currentRank null). So the practical blast radius is smaller than claimed, which keeps this at low/Consider, not higher.</sub>

### A · Manual view/edit
12 findings — 0 high · 7 med · 5 low

<details><summary>Flow map</summary>

**Entry points:** `src/app/api/manual/route.ts (GET)`, `src/app/api/manual/[id]/route.ts (PATCH)`, `src/components/mobile/MobileManual.tsx`

**Steps:**
1. Entry point: User navigates to Manual tab — `src/components/MainApp.tsx`
2. Initialize/Load manual data on first app load — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`, `src/app/api/manual/route.ts`
3. Build UI layers from confirmed entries — `src/components/mobile/manual/layer-definitions.ts`, `src/components/mobile/MobileManual.tsx`
4. Render layers as populated or empty plates — `src/components/mobile/MobileManual.tsx`, `src/components/mobile/manual/PopulatedLayer.tsx`, `src/components/mobile/manual/EmptyLayer.tsx`
5. User clicks Edit on an entry — `src/components/mobile/manual/EntryItem.tsx`
6. User modifies entry text and saves — `src/components/mobile/manual/EntryItem.tsx`
7. updateEntry hook sends PATCH to server — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`, `src/app/api/manual/[id]/route.ts`
8. Update local state optimistically — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`
9. Handle edit errors — `src/components/mobile/manual/EntryItem.tsx`, `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts`
10. User explores entry with Jove persona — `src/components/mobile/manual/EntryItem.tsx`, `src/components/MainApp.tsx`
11. Generate and share manual as PDF — `src/components/mobile/MobileManual.tsx`, `src/lib/utils/generate-manual-pdf.ts`, `src/lib/utils/share-manual.ts`
12. Prepare manual context for LLM prompt — `src/lib/persona/manual-context.ts`
13. Database schema for manual entries — `src/lib/types.ts`

**External deps:** Supabase (admin client, auth.getUser) · Anthropic (Jove persona via /api/chat) · jsPDF (PDF generation) · Navigator.share (Web Share API) · PostHog analytics

**Existing tests:** `src/lib/persona/manual-context.test.ts (64+ test cases for prepareManualContext, compressManualEntry, prepareManualContextBlocks)`, `src/db-contract.e2e.test.ts:112-145 (manual_entries schema validation)`

</details>

#### Conflicts (2)

- **[MED]** Optimistic checkpoint entry carries the message id, not the real manual_entries.id — editing it during Jove's follow-up stream PATCHes a non-existent row (404) — `src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts:857-866`
  On confirm, the optimistic entry is pushed into confirmedEntries with `id: activeCheckpoint.messageId` (useChat.ts:860). But manual_entries.id is `uuid_generate_v4()` (squash_baseline.sql:251) — a brand-new UUID unrelated to the message id; the RPC returns it as `entry_id` (confirm-checkpoint.ts:678) and the SSE message_complete event does NOT carry it (sse-parser.ts MessageCompleteEvent has no entry-id field). The only path that gives the client the real id is loadManual()'s full-array replace. On a first-time confirm, loadManual() does not run until AFTER `await streamFromResponse(res)` (useChat.ts:930, then loadManual at :938) — the entire multi-second Jove follow-up stream. During that window the entry renders in MobileManual with a working Edit button, its id is the message id. Navigation to the Manual tab is not gated during streaming (handleNavigateToManual just calls setActiveView, MainApp.tsx:328-330; no isStreaming guard on the drawer nav). If the user opens the drawer and edits during the stream, EntryItem.handleSaveEdit calls onUpdateEntry(entry.id=messageId) (EntryItem.tsx:122) → PATCH /api/manual/{messageId} → the route's `.eq('id', entryId)` (manual/[id]/route.ts:145) matches no row → PGRST116 → 404 'Entry not found.' (route.ts:151-158). The edit silently fails until loadManual() swaps in the real id. Downstream consequence: a real edit is lost to a confusing 404 in a window the product itself opens (the streamed follow-up is the slow part).
  _→ Reconcile the id before the stream, not after. Either (a) move `await loadManual()` to run immediately after the confirm response is known-OK and before streamFromResponse, or (b) surface the real entry_id on the SSE message_complete event for confirmed checkpoints and patch the optimistic entry's id from it, or (c) gate Manual-tab edit affordances while isStreaming/isLoading is true so the entry can't be edited until reconciled. Verifying timing under a real Vercel stream is worth a human/integration check — the window length depends on Anthropic latency. Add a useChat test that asserts the optimistic entry id is replaced before it becomes editable, and a route test for the PATCH 404-on-stale-id path (no test exists for /api/manual/[id] today)._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed real. The optimistic confirmed entry is pushed with id: activeCheckpoint.messageId (useChat.ts:860), and buildLayers maps that straight to Entry.id (layer-definitions.ts:34), which EntryItem.handleSaveEdit passes to onUpdateEntry(entry.id) (EntryItem.tsx:122) -> PATCH /api/manual/{messageId} -> route's .eq('id', entryId) (route.ts:145) matches no row -> PGRST116 -> 404 (route.ts:151-158). The real manual_entries.id is a fresh uuid_generate_v4 returned only as entry_id from the RPC (confirm-checkpoint.ts:678); MessageCompleteEvent carries no entry id (sse-parser.ts:3-36) and finalizeMessage never reconciles it, so on a first-time confirm the only fix-up is loadManual() at useChat.ts:938 -- which runs only AFTER await streamFromResponse(res) at line 930 (the JSON early-return at 916-919 is skipped for SSE). The Manual drawer nav is ungated (MainApp.tsx:328-330, SessionDrawer.tsx:69-72) and MobileManual gets onUpdateEntry unconditionally (MainApp.tsx:414), so the entry is editable during that multi-second window. One correction: it is not silent -- updateEntry returns the 404 body 'Entry not found.' (useChat.ts:207-214) which EntryItem renders as editError (EntryItem.tsx:127, 234-247); the typed text stays in the DOM and a retry after loadManual succeeds. Holding at medium/should-fix: the window is bounded and self-healing, no server-side data loss (atomic RPC already wrote the row), and it requires the user to navigate to Manual and edit mid-stream rather than watch the follow-up. Mechanical defect, high confidence on the code; the only thing code can't tell me is how often real users hit this window (empirical/UX).</sub>
- **[LOW]** EntryItem is keyed by entry.id, so loadManual()'s id swap remounts the card mid-interaction — discards open edit/typed text and expansion state — `src/components/mobile/manual/PopulatedLayer.tsx:87`
  EntryItem is keyed by `entry.id` (PopulatedLayer.tsx:87, id derived in layer-definitions.ts:34). Because the optimistic entry's id is the message id and loadManual() replaces it with the real manual_entries.id (see the related finding), the key changes for that one entry when reconciliation lands. React treats a changed key as unmount+remount: any local state in EntryItem (editing, saving, the typed-but-unsaved contentEditable DOM, and expanded) is thrown away and re-initialized from props. So a user who expanded or began editing the freshly-confirmed entry during the follow-up stream will see it collapse / lose typed text the instant loadManual() completes. This is a state-ownership conflict: EntryItem holds interactive state the server-driven id churn silently invalidates. Lower severity than the 404 because it only resets UI state, not persisted data, but it compounds the same root cause.
  _→ Same root fix as the id-desync finding (reconcile id before the entry is interactive, or patch the optimistic id in place rather than full-replacing the array so the key for that entry stays stable). If full-replace is kept, ensure the optimistic id equals the eventual real id so the key never changes._
  <sub>verified (high): The mechanism is real and present. EntryItem is keyed by entry.id (PopulatedLayer.tsx:87), id derives from manual_entries.id with a fallback (layer-definitions.ts:34). On confirm, the optimistic row uses activeCheckpoint.messageId as its id (useChat.ts:860), but /api/manual returns the real manual_entries.id (route.ts:18-19) — a different UUID — and loadManual() replaces confirmedEntries wholesale (useChat.ts:179, called at 938). So the key for that one entry changes on first reconciliation, and React unmount+remounts EntryItem, discarding its local state (expanded/editing/saving and the contentEditable DOM, EntryItem.tsx:65-70). Nothing reconciles identity, so it is not already handled. I confirm the finding's own 'low' severity, not a blocker: it resets only UI state never persisted data; panels are always mounted via display:none (MobileLayout.tsx:73) and entries default collapsed in non-readOnly mode (EntryItem.tsx:65), so the loss requires the user to navigate to Manual and actively expand/edit the one just-confirmed entry inside the seconds-long stream+refetch window — a narrow, one-time trigger (the id is stable after the first reconciliation). The code path is certain (high confidence); how often a real user trips it is an empirical/product judgment I am inferring on, not a code fact. Same root cause as the related id-swap 404 finding; fixing id reconciliation (match optimistic row to server row, or key on a stable field) resolves both.</sub>

#### Failure paths (1)

- **[MED]** GET /api/manual returns 200 with an empty Manual on a Supabase query error; loadManual then wipes confirmed entries with no error shown — `src/app/api/manual/route.ts:17-34`
  The Supabase JS client returns {data, error} and does not throw on query errors (no .throwOnError() is used). The route never checks the error field on either query: it does `const [{ data: components }, { data: profile }] = await Promise.all([...])` then `return Response.json({ components: components || [], displayName })`. A transient read failure (connection blip, statement timeout) yields components = null → the route responds 200 with `{ components: [] }`. The catch block and recordApiError only fire on a thrown exception, which a query error is not — so the failure is invisible server-side too. Downstream: loadManual (src/lib/hooks/useChat.ts:174-188) only acts on res.ok, has no error branch, and does `setConfirmedEntries(data.components || [])`. On a transient GET error during the post-confirm reconciliation (useChat.ts:917 and :938) or on app load (useChat.ts:520), the user's Manual is replaced with an empty array and shown the EmptyLayer 'invite' copy — a returning user with many entries momentarily sees a wiped Manual and no error, which reads as data loss. Contrast with the PATCH route, which correctly distinguishes updateError and returns 500.
  _→ Check the error field on both queries in the GET route and return a 500 (via recordApiError, matching the documented error pattern in system.md) when the manual_entries read errors, so loadManual's res.ok gate keeps the existing entries instead of clearing them. Add a route test that mocks an admin-client query error and asserts a 500 (not a 200 with empty components). Confidence high — Supabase non-throwing semantics and the no-error-check code are both directly visible._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed real. src/app/api/manual/route.ts:17-29 destructures only {data} from both queries and discards the error field; with @supabase/supabase-js v2.39.3 and no .throwOnError() anywhere in the repo, a query error (statement timeout, connection blip) resolves as {data: null, error} without throwing, so the try/catch and recordApiError never fire and line 34 returns 200 with components: [] — the failure is invisible server-side too. Downstream, loadManual (useChat.ts:174-188) only checks res.ok, has no error branch, and runs setConfirmedEntries(data.components || []), so on app load (:520/:549) or post-confirm reconcile (:917/:938) a transient GET error replaces the in-memory entries with [] and renders EmptyLayer's invite copy (EmptyLayer.tsx:132) with no error shown — exactly the inconsistency the PATCH route avoids (route.ts[id]:142-165 captures updateError and returns 500). I kept severity at medium rather than high: this is client-side React state, the manual_entries rows are untouched, and the empty render is transient (the next successful loadManual restores it) — so it is a real correctness/UX-and-observability defect, not persistent data loss. The finding's word 'wipes' slightly overstates durability but the mechanism, location, and missing error handling are all accurate.</sub>

#### Test gaps (9)

- **[MED]** Zero test coverage for the entire Manual view/edit flow (both routes, updateEntry, optimistic-confirm id) — `src/app/api/manual/[id]/route.ts:38`
