The existingTests in the flow map (manual-context.test.ts, db-contract.e2e) do not touch the failure paths here. Given the two findings above are exactly the kind of bug a test would catch (id-shape mismatch on confirm; 200-on-query-error), the gap is load-bearing. The PATCH route has non-trivial validation branches (name=null clears, empty content rejected, PGRST116 → 404, summary regeneration + key_words clear on content change) that are entirely unverified — a regression in any branch ships silently.
  _→ Add a route test for PATCH /api/manual/[id] (mock requireUser + admin client per CLAUDE.md): assert 400 on empty body, 400 on empty/oversized content, name=null clears, content change sets summary via deriveSummaryFromContent and key_words=null, PGRST116 → 404, updateError → 500. Add a hook test asserting confirmCheckpoint's optimistic entry id is the reconciled manual_entries id, not messageId. This is a test-gap recommendation, not a claim the validation logic is currently wrong._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified: grep across all 44 test files for api/manual, updateEntry, and loadManual returns zero hits, and no route.test.ts exists under src/app/api/manual/ or its [id]/ subdir. useChat.test.ts imports only the pure buildChatMessageFromEvent helper (5 cases) and never touches updateEntry/loadManual/confirmCheckpoint (which exist in useChat.ts at lines 196/174/733). manual-context.test.ts covers prepareManualContext/compressManualEntry and does not even test deriveSummaryFromContent, the function PATCH calls at route.ts:126. db-contract.e2e only asserts manual_entries insert shapes, not GET/PATCH failure paths. The PATCH route's branches (name=null clear at :80, too-long 400 at :86, empty-content 400 at :110, summary-regen+key_words-clear at :126-127, PGRST116->404 at :151, error->500 at :160) and the manualEditHour rate limit (:49) are all unverified, and the upstream concerns are corroborated in code (GET at :17-34 discards the query error and returns 200 with components:[]; useChat.updateEntry reconciles by e.id===entry.id at :218). This is an accurate mechanical test-gap finding, not a design opinion. Medium is right: missing coverage on a branchy route with a known-bug class, but a gap rather than a shipped defect, so non-blocking on its own.</sub>
- **[MED]** PATCH /api/manual/[id] ownership scoping (user_id) is completely untested — `src/app/api/manual/[id]/route.ts:142-158`
  The only thing preventing user A from editing user B's manual entry is the `.eq("user_id", user.id)` clause at line 146 (createAdminClient bypasses RLS, per system.md Three Supabase Clients). There is no test that the update filter is scoped to the authenticated user's id, and no test that a non-matching (id, user_id) pair returns 404 via the PGRST116 branch at line 151. This is the security-critical path for this flow and has zero coverage. The phone route test (route.test.ts:162-177) shows the harness can capture filter/patch values, so this is directly assertable.

Assert that PATCH issues an update filtered by BOTH id===params.id AND user_id===authenticated user.id (never another id). Separately, set the single() response to {data:null,error:{code:"PGRST116"}} and assert status 404 with body {error:"Entry not found."} and that no further write leaks entry existence._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed real. At src/app/api/manual/[id]/route.ts:142-148 the PATCH uses createAdminClient (service-role, bypasses RLS per docs/system.md:184 and admin.ts:14), so the sole guard against user A editing user B's entry is the .eq("user_id", user.id) filter at line 146, with the PGRST116 -> 404 branch at lines 150-158. An exhaustive grep across src/**/*.test.ts found no test importing this route, referencing manualEditHour, or asserting on the ownership filter — the only manual_entries tests (db-contract.e2e, chat/route, confirm-checkpoint.e2e) exercise schema shape or other routes, not this ownership scoping. The gap is directly assertable: src/app/api/user/phone/route.test.ts:72-74 captures .update() patch values into an asserted array, the same pattern that would cover the missing 404 and user_id-scoping assertions. (Finding's citation route.test.ts:162-177 is actually src/app/api/user/phone/route.test.ts, but the harness-capability claim is accurate.) I refine high to medium: the in-code control is correct and present today, so this is a regression-risk test gap on a security-critical single-clause defense, not a live vulnerability — real should-fix, but below the security-hole/known-broken-path bar that high implies.</sub>
- **[MED]** PATCH returns 401 unauthenticated and 429 rate-limited — neither branch tested — `src/app/api/manual/[id]/route.ts:44-50`

Refined from high to medium: this is a missing-test gap, not a code defect — the gates are correctly implemented and would work in production, so it is a should-fix to track (a regression removing a gate would go uncaught), not a ship-blocker.</sub>
- **[MED]** Content edit must regenerate summary and clear key_words — side effect untested — `src/app/api/manual/[id]/route.ts:122-128`

No test asserts the update patch carries the regenerated summary and key_words:null. Equally untested: a name-only edit (body.content undefined) must NOT touch summary or key_words.

- **[MED]** PATCH validation branches (empty body, oversize/empty content, oversize/invalid name, wrong types) untested — `src/app/api/manual/[id]/route.ts:65-135`

Second.' -> 'First.'; a 240-char no-punctuation string -> returned unchanged (length 240); a 241-char string -> ends with '...' and total length 240 (237 + 3). **State touched:** Client state: MobileSettings phoneState, linkedPhone, phoneInput, codeInput, linkedService, phoneError, phoneBusy; PersonaModePicker local selection array; dyslexic font CSS attribute and localStorage 'mywalnut.persona-dyslexic' · Server state: Supabase profiles.persona_modes (read/write), phone_numbers.phone, phone_numbers.verified, phone_numbers.otp_code (hash), phone_numbers.otp_expires_at, phone_numbers.otp_attempts, phone_numbers.linked_at; messaging_events audit table (redacted content per ADR-037) · Auth state: requireUser() validates session for all routes

**Existing tests:** `src/app/api/user/persona-modes/route.test.ts (validatePersonaModes unit tests, PATCH route integration tests)`, `src/app/api/user/phone/route.test.ts (POST OTP send, POST verify, DELETE disconnect, otp_attempts cap enforcement)`, `src/lib/persona/persona-mode-toggle.test.ts (togglePersonaMode logic, exclusivity enforcement)`, `src/lib/phone-otp.test.ts (OTP generation, hashing, expiry checks)`, `src/lib/hooks/usePersonaDyslexicFont.test.ts (font sync logic)`, `src/db-contract.e2e.test.ts (profiles: persona_modes update test)`

**Notes:** PERSONA MODES STACK NOTE: As noted in instructions, persona modes stack — autistic+adhd can both be selected together. There is no standalone 'audhd' mode; togglePersonaMode enforces the four discrete modes (autistic, adhd, dyslexic, general), and general is exclusive. The validator dedupes the array and enforces that general cannot combine with any neurotype. PHONE OTP SECURITY: Raw 6-digit code is hashed SHA-256 before storage. Per-phone rate limiting (5/hour send, 5/10min verify) is Upstash-based (fails open dev/preview). Per-row otp_attempts counter (max 5, restored 2026-05-19 via migration 20260519000000) caps brute force offline. Verify-route increments on wrong code, resets to 0 on success or fresh send. NO TEST COVERAGE for account delete route. MESSAGING ROUTING: sendMessage unified interface (lib/messaging/send.ts) routes 1:1 sends to active provider (MESSAGING_PROVIDER env) or Linq group chat path. Audit trail redacted per ADR-037: content is never logged, only contentKind-based markers (e.g., '[OTP_SEND]'). DYSLEXIC FONT: PersonaModePicker calls applyDyslexicFont() synchronously on toggle before PATCH completes, so UI feels instant. FOUC script in layout prevents flash; usePersonaDyslexicFont reconciles on mount and handles cross-tab storage sync."

</details>

#### Security (2)

- **[MED]** OTP-write error logging can leak the phone number into logs (violates 'never log phone numbers') — `src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:136, src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:153`
  On an OTP upsert failure the route logs the full Supabase/PostgREST error object via `details: updateError` (line 136) and `details: insertError` (line 153). A unique-constraint violation on `phone_numbers_phone_key UNIQUE (phone)` (squash_baseline.sql:438) produces a PostgREST error whose `details` field is the literal `Key (phone)=(+1XXXXXXXXXX) already exists.` — i.e. it contains the raw E.164 phone number. Logging the whole error object therefore writes a user phone number to the server logs, which the CLAUDE.md Security Rule 'Never log user message content, phone numbers, or auth tokens' forbids. This is reachable in normal operation: it fires whenever a user tries to (re)link a number already held by another row. Confidence: high that the rule is violated; medium on how often the unique-violation path is actually hit in practice.
  _→ Drop `details: updateError`/`details: insertError` and log only `updateError.code` and `updateError.message` (the message string for a unique violation is generic and does not embed the value; the `details`/`hint` fields are the ones that do). Mirror the safe pattern already used in persona-modes/route.ts:43 which logs only `message`._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed real. The code at src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:136 and :153 logs the full Supabase error object via `details: updateError` / `details: insertError`. The unique constraint `phone_numbers_phone_key UNIQUE (phone)` exists (squash_baseline.sql:438), and on a violation PostgREST populates the error's `details` field with Postgres's diagnostic `Key (phone)=(+1XXXXXXXXXX) already exists.`, which embeds the raw E.164 number — directly violating the CLAUDE.md Security Rule "Never log user message content, phone numbers, or auth tokens" (CLAUDE.md:100). The path is genuinely reachable: when the requesting user has no row (INSERT branch) but another user holds that phone UNVERIFIED, the pre-checks at lines 68-95 do not fire (the 409 requires verified===true and the no-op requires the same user+verified), so execution falls through to an INSERT that collides on the unique phone key; a concurrent-insert TOCTOU window between the line-68 SELECT and the line-141 INSERT/UPDATE is a second, lower-frequency trigger. Medium severity is correct: logs are server-internal (not user-exposed) and the leaked datum is a single phone number on an error path, not bulk PII or a secret. Fix: log `code`/`message`/IDs only, or strip `details`/`hint` before logging (note verify/route.ts:130 and webhooks/sendblue/route.ts:120,178,315 share the same `details: <errorObject>` pattern and should be swept for the same leak where the underlying table contains phone numbers).</sub>
- **[LOW]** Rate limiters fail open on preview deployments, leaving only the per-row otp_attempts cap as OTP abuse protection — `src/lib/rate-limit.ts:34-43`
  The module throws (hard-fails) when Upstash env vars are missing ONLY when `VERCEL_ENV === 'production'`. Preview deployments fail open: `phoneOtpSendHour` and `phoneOtpVerifyTenMin` become no-ops (checkLimit returns ALLOW_OPEN at rate-limit.ts:103). On a preview URL an attacker can request unlimited OTP sends to an arbitrary number (SMS-bombing a victim) and the only verify-side backstop is the per-row `otp_attempts` cap of 5 (phone-otp.ts:29, verify/route.ts:82). The send side has no DB-level cap at all in that mode, so OTP send-spam is unbounded on preview. This is documented as intentional (rate-limit.ts:1-7, 31-33) and production is protected, but preview deployments are publicly reachable and authenticate real Supabase sessions, so the exposure is non-zero. Confidence: high on the mechanism; this is a deployment-surface risk, not a production-code defect.
  _→ Either gate the OTP send/verify routes behind a hard check that refuses when no limiter is configured outside local dev (treat preview like production for these two endpoints specifically), or add an unconditional per-row/per-phone DB send counter so the send side has a backstop even when Upstash is absent. At minimum, confirm preview env never serves these routes to untrusted traffic._
  <sub>verified (high): The mechanism is real and every line citation checks out: with Upstash env vars unset on a preview deploy, redis is null (rate-limit.ts:23), the throw at rate-limit.ts:35 does NOT fire because VERCEL_ENV !== 'production', so phoneOtpSendHour/phoneOtpVerifyTenMin are null and checkLimit returns ALLOW_OPEN (rate-limit.ts:103). The send route (src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:97-101) then has its only abuse gate no-op'd, and I confirmed the rest of that POST handler has NO DB-level send cap — only the verify route keeps an Upstash-independent backstop via otp_attempts (verify/route.ts:81-91, OTP_MAX_ATTEMPTS=5). So the send-vs-verify asymmetry the finding describes is accurate. However it is low severity and arguably a deployment-surface note rather than a code defect: the send route still requires an authenticated Supabase session (requireUser, route.ts:48 — not an open relay), only (re)issues an OTP on the attacker's own single phone_numbers row scoped by user_id (lines 114/131), and refuses numbers already verified to anyone (lines 68-95), so 'unlimited sends to an arbitrary victim' is narrower than stated; the fail-open-on-preview behavior is explicitly documented as intentional (rate-limit.ts:1-7, 25-33), production is hard-protected by the throw, OTP routes fall outside the CLAUDE.md 'rate-limit routes that call Anthropic' rule, and the worst case is bounded SMS spend with no path to a false verification. The finding itself concedes it is a deployment-surface risk, not a production-code defect, which is the right read.</sub>

#### Conflicts (5)

- **[MED]** OTP send race: read-then-write with no unique key on user_id can create duplicate phone_numbers rows or lose the OTP write under concurrent POSTs — `src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:111-157`
  The send route does a read-then-write: SELECT id WHERE user_id (line 111-115), then branch to UPDATE (line 122) or INSERT (line 141). There is NO unique constraint on phone_numbers.user_id — the schema only has UNIQUE(phone) (supabase/migrations/20260417000000_squash_baseline.sql:434-446) and a plain btree index phone_numbers_user_id_idx (line 552-555). Two near-simultaneous POSTs for a brand-new user (e.g. the user double-taps 'Send code', or the 'Resend code' button which calls the SAME handleConnectPhone with NO new-OTP-specific guard — only the shared phoneBusy flag, which does not protect across a fast network where the first response already returned) both read userRow=null and both take the INSERT branch. The second insert fails the UNIQUE(phone) constraint and returns 500 'Failed to send code' to the user even though an OTP was already sent and the row exists — a confusing contradictory state (user got a code but the UI shows an error). Worse case: if the two requests carry different phone numbers (user edits the field between taps), the first inserts phone A, the second's UNIQUE(phone) check passes for phone B but now there are effectively two writes racing on the same user with only the phone-uniqueness guard — the verify route later selects WHERE user_id AND phone (verify/route.ts:57-62) and could match whichever row won, while GET /api/user/phone uses maybeSingle() WHERE user_id (route.ts:24-28) which THROWS if two rows exist for one user. The 'one row per user' invariant the code comments assert (route.ts:108) is not enforced by the database. ADR-027 chose retry-on-failure for the analogous getOrCreateConversation race but that mitigation was never applied here.
  _→ Either add a UNIQUE constraint on phone_numbers.user_id (the code already assumes one row per user via maybeSingle) and convert the send path to an upsert keyed on user_id, or apply the ADR-027 retry-on-failure pattern: catch the 23505 unique violation and re-query. A real concurrency test (two POSTs racing) cannot be written against the current mock; recommend a human-run integration test against a real Supabase instance, or at minimum a unit test asserting the 23505 path is handled rather than surfaced as a bare 500._
  <sub>verified (high): The structural defect is genuinely present and verified: the send route does an unguarded read-then-write (src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:111-157), the schema has only UNIQUE(phone) and a non-unique phone_numbers_user_id_idx (squash_baseline.sql:438, 555) with no UNIQUE(user_id), no trigger, and no application race guard — so the "one row per user" invariant the comment at route.ts:108 asserts is unenforced, and ADR-027's retry-on-failure (used for the identical getOrCreateConversation race) was never applied here. The realistically-reachable consequence, however, is narrower than the finding claims: a fast double-tap of "Send code" by a brand-new user can make the losing concurrent INSERT violate UNIQUE(phone) and return a spurious 500 "Failed to send code" even though the OTP was sent (a recoverable UX papercut, no data loss). The two-rows-per-user worst case requires editing the phone field AND landing a second tap inside React's sub-frame batching window (not humanly achievable), the GET/verify maybeSingle() throw only triggers if two rows somehow exist, and the "Resend code" path only renders in phoneState==="code" after a row exists (UPDATE-vs-UPDATE, idempotent) — so it does not create the new-user duplicate. Real and worth tracking as a should-fix that conflicts with ADR-027, but high severity is inflated. Confidence on the unguarded server code is high; whether two POSTs actually interleave under Vercel scheduling is a timing question best confirmed by a concurrent-request test.</sub>
- **[MED]** Verify route: concurrent double-submit can promote twice and send two greeting texts + insert two greeting messages (no idempotency on the success side effects) — `src/app/api/user/phone/verify/route.ts:93-176`
  handleVerifyCode is guarded only by the shared phoneBusy flag (MobileSettings.tsx:162-184), which is per-request and does not prevent a second submit once the first request's promise is in flight on a slow connection, nor a retry from a flaky network. The route is not idempotent on success: it compares hash (line 94), then unconditionally UPDATEs verified=true and clears the OTP (line 116-125), then ALWAYS sends INITIAL_GREETING via sendMessage (line 140) and inserts a greeting row into messages (line 170). Two requests carrying the same correct code can both pass the hash check (the UPDATE that clears otp_code is not gated on the row still being unverified — there is no WHERE verified=false guard), so both proceed to the greeting side effects: the user receives the welcome SMS twice and two assistant greeting rows land in their conversation history. The otp_attempts/expiry guards protect the wrong-code brute-force path but do nothing for a duplicated correct submission. This is a duplicate-send idempotency gap, exactly the class flagged for this lens.
  _→ Gate the promotion UPDATE on the row still being unverified (e.g. .eq('id', row.id).eq('verified', false)) and only fire the greeting when that update actually affected a row (check the returned rowcount / use .select() and confirm a row came back). Add a unit test: two verify calls with the same correct code -> exactly one verified=true write and exactly one sendMessage(greeting). The greeting send is already documented as best-effort/non-fatal, so making it fire-once is low-risk._
  <sub>verified (high): Real and currently present. The promote UPDATE at src/app/api/user/phone/verify/route.ts:116-125 is gated only by .eq("id", row.id) with no WHERE verified=false / otp_code-is-null compare-and-swap, and the read (lines 57-62) and write are separate statements, so two concurrent requests with the same correct code both pass the hash check (line 94) and both run the unconditional greeting sendMessage (line 140) and messages insert (line 170); the messages table (squash_baseline.sql:269-282) has no unique constraint to dedupe the second row. The rate limiter (phoneOtpVerifyTenMin, 5/10min sliding window, rate-limit.ts:81) counts both requests as allowed and fails open when Upstash is unprovisioned (the current posture per ADR-038's reversal), and the otp_attempts/expiry guards only protect the wrong-code path (counter resets to 0 on success, route.ts:122). Client guard (MobileSettings.tsx:162-184, button disabled at 615) is a per-request phoneBusy boolean that does not survive a network retry or pre-render double-tap. Severity held at medium: impact is a duplicate welcome SMS (which still carries the STOP/HELP footer) plus a duplicate assistant history row — annoyance, not data loss/security/corruption (verified state is idempotent). High confidence on the mechanical defect from static reading; the production hit-rate of the interleave is timing-dependent and would need a real concurrency test to quantify. Finding's path string is wrong (actual file src/components/mobile/MobileSettings.tsx) but its line numbers are correct.</sub>
- **[MED]** persona_modes is read independently by three surfaces with one stale after a toggle; PersonaModePicker and usePersonaDyslexicFont hold separate copies that desync on cross-tab or external change — `src/lib/hooks/usePersonaDyslexicFont.ts:61-88, src/components/mobile/settings/PersonaModePicker.tsx:62-100`
  Three client surfaces each load persona_modes from Supabase on their own: PersonaModePicker (its own getUser + profiles select, lines 74-78), usePersonaDyslexicFont (its own getUser + profiles select, lines 66-79, mounted once in layout), and the server-side loadConversationContext on every turn. The picker is the only writer (PATCH at line 122). On a successful toggle the picker updates its OWN state and calls applyDyslexicFont (lines 118-119), and the font hook happens to observe the dyslexic bit via localStorage. But the font hook only reconciles persona_modes from the server ONCE on mount (line 61) — if persona_modes changes in another tab via the picker, the storage event (line 92-100) only carries the dyslexic boolean, so the dyslexic font syncs cross-tab but the OPEN picker in the other tab keeps its now-stale selected[] array (it has no storage listener and no refetch). A user toggling modes in tab A then toggling again in tab B's stale picker will PATCH based on a stale base array, silently clobbering tab A's change (last-write-wins on the whole array, not a merge). Because MobileSettings never unmounts (MobileLayout.tsx:73 uses display:none, not conditional render), the picker's stale state also persists for the entire session — there is no remount to re-read the authoritative value after, e.g., an external/admin change to persona_modes. This is the hook-holds-state-the-server-also-owns desync the lens targets.
  _→ Lowest-cost fix: have PersonaModePicker re-read persona_modes when Settings becomes active (thread isActive in, refetch on the false->true edge) so a stale picker re-anchors. A more complete fix gives the picker a cross-tab listener on a persona_modes storage key. Given single-founder beta usage, document the last-write-wins limitation if not fixed; a multi-tab regression test is the right guard before this matters at scale._
  <sub>verified (high): Real desync-and-clobber. PersonaModePicker loads persona_modes once on mount (PersonaModePicker.tsx:62-100) and never remounts — MobileLayout.tsx:73 keeps all panels mounted via display:none, and the picker has no storage listener, no realtime, and no focus/isActive-driven refetch (the only isActive effect, MobileSettings.tsx:113-127, gates the phone-number fetch, not the picker). The PATCH does a whole-array update with no merge (route.ts:39), so a second same-account tab toggling its stale picker PATCHes a stale base array via togglePersonaMode and silently drops the other tab's saved mode. The asymmetry is exactly as stated: the dyslexic font reconciles cross-tab via usePersonaDyslexicFont.ts:92-100's storage listener, but the picker's selected[] does not, so the font flips while the checkboxes go stale. Refined to medium, not high, because it requires two concurrent sessions on one account (or an external/admin write while a session is live) — uncommon on a mobile-first single-session app — and the loss is recoverable by re-toggling; but it is a genuine mechanical bug (silent loss of a setting that drives Jove's voice every turn per route.ts:14-18), not a design opinion. Finding cites the wrong path for the layout (actual: src/components/layout/MobileLayout.tsx:73, not src/components/mobile/MobileLayout.tsx), but the mechanism and line number are correct.</sub>
- **[LOW]** Phone status fetched exactly once per session via hasFetchedPhoneRef; GET reuses the same ref so the panel can show stale 'linked'/'unlinked' state after a same-session relink or external change — `src/components/mobile/MobileSettings.tsx:111-127`
  MobileSettings mounts once and never unmounts (MobileLayout.tsx:73, display:none toggling). hasFetchedPhoneRef (line 111) guards GET /api/user/phone to fire exactly once per mount — i.e. once per session. After that, phoneState is driven purely by local optimistic transitions in the handlers (handleVerifyCode sets 'linked', handleDisconnect sets 'unlinked'). This is internally consistent for actions taken in THIS tab, but the local client state is the only source of truth after the initial fetch: if the phone link changes out of band (a second tab/device disconnects, or the verify side-effect partially fails), this panel never re-reconciles against the server because the ref is permanently true and the panel never remounts. Also note linkedPhone is set to the locally-typed phoneToSend on verify success (line 176) rather than the server's normalized value, so the displayed number is the user's raw input formatting, not the canonical E.164 the server stored — a minor client/server display desync. Lower severity than the write races because it is read-side and self-corrects on full reload, but it is a genuine client-owns-state-the-server-also-owns gap given the never-unmount layout.
  _→ Re-fetch phone status on the isActive false->true transition (drop the permanent ref in favor of an effect keyed on isActive, or keep the ref but reset it when isActive flips) so reopening Settings re-anchors to the server. Set linkedPhone from the verify response's canonical phone rather than the typed input. Test: relink in a second context, reopen Settings, assert it reflects server truth._
  <sub>verified (high): The mechanical premise is fully confirmed: MobileLayout.tsx:62-81 renders all four panels and toggles only display:block/none + hidden at line 73, so MobileSettings never unmounts; hasFetchedPhoneRef (MobileSettings.tsx:111-114) is permanently true after the first Settings open and the effect deps are [isActive], so GET /api/user/phone fires exactly once per session and there is genuinely no re-fetch/focus/visibility re-sync — after that, phoneState/linkedPhone are driven only by the local optimistic handlers, so an out-of-band change (second device, lost verify response, support unlink) shows stale state until full reload. That is a real client-owns-state-the-server-also-owns gap, exactly as described. I refine to low, not medium, because the blast radius is cosmetic: the displayed value is a phone-link status label, the disconnect/relink handlers re-POST/DELETE to the server unconditionally rather than trusting phoneState to gate any action, and the single-device user never sees self-induced staleness. The secondary 'display desync' sub-claim is technically true at the variable level (verify sets linkedPhone=pendingPhone raw input at line 176 while the server stores normalizePhone E.164) but is masked in practice: both values render through formatPhoneDisplay (lines 95-104, 680), which strips to digits and reformats, so any number that actually validates (11 digits) collapses to the identical (XXX) XXX-XXXX string — no user-visible difference. No data-integrity or security consequence; self-corrects on reload.</sub>
- **[LOW]** service_type is read by GET /api/user/phone but never written by any code path, so a Sendblue-linked user's CONNECTED badge never shows a provider — `src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:37 and src/app/api/user/phone/verify/route.ts:116-125`
  GET /api/user/phone returns serviceType from phone_numbers.service_type (route.ts:37), and MobileSettings renders 'CONNECTED · {SERVICE}' from it (MobileSettings.tsx:691). The verify route, which is the only path that links a phone, never sets service_type (the promotion patch at verify/route.ts:116-125 omits it). A repo-wide grep confirms service_type is never written anywhere in src/ (only the Linq group message-router updates linq_chat_id, not service_type). So under the Sendblue 1:1 default (ADR-035), a freshly-linked phone always has service_type=null and the UI shows bare 'CONNECTED'. Not a correctness bug — the badge degrades gracefully via the ternary at line 691 — but it is a documented field (schema-map/page.tsx:301 says it 'tracks which provider routes for this number') that no write path populates, a quiet client/server contract gap.
  _→ Either set service_type on the verify promotion to the active provider (read from MESSAGING_PROVIDER / the sendMessage result's provider), or drop the column and the UI branch if provider-per-number is not actually tracked. Confirm which is intended with the founder before changing — this is a product/observability call, not a pure bug._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed against the code. GET /api/user/phone (route.ts:37) returns serviceType from phone_numbers.service_type, and MobileSettings (src/components/mobile/MobileSettings.tsx:120,691) binds it and renders 'CONNECTED · {SERVICE}' or bare 'CONNECTED'. The verify route's promotion patch (verify/route.ts:116-125) sets verified/otp fields/linked_at but omits service_type, and a repo-wide grep shows service_type is never written anywhere in src/ (the only phone_numbers messaging writes are linq_chat_id at message-router.ts:189 and linq.ts:116). The migration (20260417000000:299) defines service_type with no DEFAULT, so every row is NULL, and schema-map/page.tsx:301 documents it as the provider-routing field with no populating writer — a real, present client/server contract gap. It is cosmetic only: routing is driven by the MESSAGING_PROVIDER flag in send.ts, not this column, and the badge degrades gracefully via the line-691 ternary, so low severity is correct. One refinement: there is a second identical dead reader at src/app/api/settings/link-phone/route.ts:18,29 that the finding missed.</sub>

#### Failure paths (6)

- **[MED]** account/delete is a multi-step best-effort cascade with no transaction; a mid-cascade failure leaves orphaned user data and the route can still return ok or a misleading 500 — `src/app/api/account/delete/route.ts:16-43`
  The delete performs six sequential admin operations (select convs, delete messages, delete conversations, delete manual_entries, delete linq_group_chats, delete profiles, then deleteUser). None of the intermediate .delete() calls check their error result — only the final deleteUser is checked (line 38-39). If any earlier delete fails (connection blip, query error) the code proceeds anyway. Two contradictory outcomes are possible: (a) the data deletes fail but deleteUser succeeds -> the auth user is gone but messages/conversations/manual_entries rows persist orphaned (their user_id FK is ON DELETE CASCADE from profiles per squash_baseline.sql:657, and profiles cascades from auth.users — so in practice the cascade MIGHT clean up, meaning the explicit deletes are partly redundant; but linq_group_chats is SET NULL on profile delete per the route's own comment, so a failed explicit linq delete leaves a row with owner_user_id=null that the privacy policy says should be gone); (b) deleteUser fails after the explicit deletes succeeded -> returns 500 'Failed to delete account' but the user's conversations/manual are already destroyed, so a retry from the client (the UI just does fetch then redirects, MobileSettings.tsx:222-226, with no error handling) operates on a half-deleted account. There is no test coverage for this route (confirmed: no account/delete test file in the flow map). For a privacy-promise delete path this is the failure path most worth hardening.
  _→ Reorder so deleteUser (the cascade root) runs first and rely on the FK CASCADE, OR check each delete's error and fail closed with a clear partial-failure status, OR wrap in a Postgres function/RPC that runs in a transaction. Add tests: (1) deleteUser error returns 500 AND the response signals data may be partially removed; (2) linq_group_chats delete error is surfaced rather than swallowed. Given this destroys user data irreversibly and has zero tests, recommend a human review before beta._
  <sub>verified (high): Verified against source: route.ts:16-35 issues five unchecked `await admin.from(...).delete()` calls (messages, conversations, manual_entries, linq_group_chats, profiles); only deleteUser at line 38-39 checks `error`, so any earlier failure proceeds silently. The durable orphan is real: linq_group_chats.owner_user_id is ON DELETE SET NULL from profiles (squash_baseline.sql:609), so a failed explicit linq delete (line 32) plus a successful deleteUser leaves a surviving row with owner_user_id=NULL — exactly the privacy-policy violation the route's own comment (lines 29-31) describes. Scenario (b) is also real: deleteUser failing after the explicit deletes destroys the Manual/conversations yet the client handleDeleteAccount (MobileSettings.tsx:222-226) does `await fetch` with no .ok check and redirects to /login regardless, so the user can log back into a wiped-but-surviving account and never sees the 500. No test exists (only route.ts in account/delete). One nuance: the conversations/messages/manual_entries orphan is largely backstopped by the auth.users→profiles→conversations→messages CASCADE chain (lines 665/593/649), so the truly unrecoverable remnant is linq_group_chats, not the whole dataset. Severity medium is correct — privacy-promise path, no transaction/no error gating, no tests, but low-frequency and partly cascade-mitigated; not a guaranteed-on-every-call data loss that would warrant high.</sub>
- **[MED]** Account-delete explicit-delete order contradicts the source_message_id FK and swallows the error — `src/app/api/account/delete/route.ts:23-27`
  The route deletes in the order messages (line 23) → conversations (line 24) → manual_entries (line 27). But manual_entries.source_message_id references messages(id) with NO ON DELETE clause = RESTRICT (supabase/migrations/20260417000000_squash_baseline.sql:633). For any user who has confirmed manual entries, the messages delete at line 23 (and the conversations delete at line 24, which cascades to messages) is REJECTED by the FK. Neither delete's error is checked — the route ignores the returned `error`. Deletion currently only completes because line 35 deletes the profiles row, which cascades conversations→messages, and by then manual_entries are already gone (line 27), so the cascade succeeds. Net effect: lines 23-24 are silent no-ops for any real user with a populated manual, and account deletion correctness depends entirely on the profiles cascade. This is the exact constraint dev-reset documents and orders correctly ('manual_entries must be deleted BEFORE messages', src/app/api/dev-reset/route.ts:17-20). The danger is twofold: (1) the route reports the wrong reason it works, so a future 'add error checking to make delete robust' change would start returning 500 for every user with manual entries; (2) if the profiles cascade is ever altered, deletion breaks silently with a {ok:true} response. No test exists for this route (confirmed: no test file under src/app/api/account/).
  _→ Reorder to match dev-reset: delete manual_entries (and manual_changelog) BEFORE messages. Check the `error` on each delete and return 500 if any fails, the same way dev-reset wraps its sequence in try/catch. Add a route test with a mocked admin client that asserts delete order and that an FK-style error on an intermediate delete surfaces as a non-200 rather than {ok:true}. - **[MED]** OTP send overwrites a verified phone link to verified=false BEFORE the send; a send failure destroys the prior link — `src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:117-177`

The early no-op guard at lines 88-95 only fires when the SAME phone is already verified — it does not cover a user who is verified on phone X and submits a different phone Y. In that case existingForPhone (looked up by the new phone Y at line 68) is typically null, so control reaches line 117, finds the user's row for phone X (verified=true), and overwrites it to phone Y / verified=false / new OTP. If sendMessage then returns FAILED, the user's previously working link to phone X is gone and replaced by an unverified, unsendable phone Y. The DB write is not rolled back on send failure. In src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts the existing-row UPDATE (lines 122-131) is keyed only by user_id and unconditionally sets phone=Y, verified=false, otp_code=<new hash>, otp_attempts=0 with no guard sparing a currently-verified row; it commits as its own Supabase HTTP transaction before sendMessage is called at line 161, and sendMessage is confirmed non-throwing (src/lib/messaging/send.ts:10-13,126,161-166), so the FAILED→502 path at 167-177 returns with the row already destroyed and no rollback. The no-op guard at 88-95 only fires when existingForPhone (looked up by the NEW phone Y at 68-72) is the same user and verified; when a user is verified on X and submits a different Y, Y is unused so existingForPhone is null and the guard is skipped. Schema confirms phone_numbers has no unique constraint on user_id (only non-unique phone_numbers_user_id_idx) but the code enforces one-row-per-user via maybeSingle and never inserts a second row, so the single row updated is the verified-X row — its loss breaks the user's text channel (idx_phone_lookup is WHERE verified=true) with no code delivered to recover. Medium is correct: recoverable via re-request/re-verify, no cross-user or security impact (the UNIQUE(phone)+verified-owner refusal at 74-86 still protects other users), but a genuine partial-write that destroys a working verified credential. Note: the demotion of X to verified=false happens even on a successful send of Y (before the user proves ownership of Y); the failed-send case is just the strictly worse sub-case.</sub>

- **[LOW]** Unverified phone owned by another user produces a confusing 500 rather than a clean refusal; takeover is blocked only by the DB unique constraint — `src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:74-86`
  The explicit takeover guard (lines 74-86) only refuses when the existing row for that phone has `verified === true`. When User A has started linking a number but never verified (verified=false), User B submitting the same number falls through both guards (74-78 requires verified=true; 88-93 requires same user) into the upsert. B's INSERT (line 141) or UPDATE (line 117) then sets `phone` to A's number, which collides with `phone_numbers_phone_key UNIQUE (phone)` (squash_baseline.sql:438) and returns a generic 500 'Failed to send code'. The good news, verified by tracing: the OTP is sent at line 161 AFTER the DB write, so the failed write returns before any code is sent — there is NO victim SMS-spam and NO takeover here. The issue is that the protection is an incidental side effect of the unique constraint rather than an explicit, intentional refusal, and the user sees a 500 instead of a meaningful message. Confidence: high (traced against the actual schema constraint and statement order).
  _→ Extend the existing 409 guard at lines 74-86 to also refuse (or surface a clear 'this number is being verified by another account' message) when `existingForPhone.user_id !== user.id` regardless of `verified`. Add a route test for the 'phone held by a different unverified user' case asserting no OTP send and a non-500 response._
  <sub>verified (high): The finding's mechanics are fully accurate, traced against the actual code and schema. Guard 1 (src/app/api/user/phone/route.ts:74-78) refuses only when the other user's row has verified===true; Guard 2 (lines 89-93) no-ops only for the same user with verified===true. A second user submitting another user's UNVERIFIED number falls through both into the INSERT (line 143) — or UPDATE (line 124) if they already have a row — both setting phone to the existing number, which violates phone_numbers_phone_key UNIQUE (phone) (squash_baseline.sql:438) and returns the generic {error:'Failed to send code'} 500 at line 138/155 instead of a clean 409. The finding's safety conclusion is also correct and load-bearing: sendMessage is at line 161, AFTER the failed DB write returns, so no OTP is sent to the victim — confirmed NO SMS spam and NO takeover (verified=true is set only in /verify after an owner-scoped hash match). This is a pure UX defect (confusing 500 vs. a meaningful refusal), correctly self-rated low — a Consider, not a Should-fix: the trigger is narrow (two users racing the same unverified number) and nothing is leaked or corrupted. The only slight overstatement is calling the protection purely 'incidental' — the takeover guard for the verified case is deliberate (lines 66-67); the unique constraint is the backstop specifically for the unverified-collision case.</sub>

#### Test gaps (8)

- **[MED]** account/delete route has zero test coverage — auth scoping and partial-cascade failure path both untested — `src/app/api/account/delete/route.ts:6-48`
  This destructive route (full cascade delete + admin.auth.admin.deleteUser) has no test file anywhere (confirmed: find src/app/api/account -name '*.test.ts' returns nothing; the db-contract.e2e.test.ts:105 deleteUser call is e2e teardown, not a route test). Two security/correctness branches are untested: (1) Auth scoping — that every delete is filtered by the AUTHENTICATED user.id, never an attacker-supplied id, and that 401 short-circuits before any delete fires. (2) The partial-failure path: the five cascade deletes (messages, conversations, manual_entries, linq_group_chats, profiles) at lines 23-35 are NOT error-checked; only deleteUser at line 38 is. If a cascade delete silently errors but deleteUser succeeds, the route still returns { ok: true } (line 43) while orphaned user data survives in messages/conversations/manual_entries — a privacy-policy violation that returns success. The catch block at line 44 only triggers on a thrown exception, which the Supabase query builder does not throw for query errors.
  _→ Add src/app/api/account/delete/route.test.ts mirroring the phone route mock harness. Assert: (a) unauthenticated → 401 and NO delete/deleteUser calls fire; (b) happy path issues deletes scoped to user.id (eq filter value === authenticated id) on all five tables AND calls deleteUser(user.id), returns 200; (c) deleteUser error → 500 with { error }. Separately, flag to the founder that the route returning ok:true while cascade deletes silently error is a real failure-path bug (the test for it should assert the route does NOT report success when a cascade delete returns an error — but that assertion will fail against current code, so it documents a fix, not current behavior). I am confident this is a genuine gap; whether the silent-success behavior is acceptable is a product/privacy call worth a human decision._
  <sub>verified (high): Confirmed: no test file exists anywhere under src/app/api/account (find returns nothing), no test references account/delete, and the two deleteUser hits in tests (db-contract.e2e.test.ts:105, confirm-checkpoint.e2e.test.ts:89) are self-user teardown, not route tests — and this codebase DOES test route handlers (11 such files), so it's a genuine gap. The partial-cascade branch is a real present defect: the five .delete() calls at lines 23,24,27,32,35 drop their {error}; supabase-js v2 (^2.39.3) returns errors as values rather than throwing, so the catch at line 44 won't fire on a query error, and the route returns {ok:true} (line 43) while orphaned data can survive — a documented privacy-promise violation (state.md:96). I refine high→medium because (a) the auth-scoping branch the finding flags is correct-by-construction — requireUser() 401s before any admin client is created and every delete is filtered by the authenticated user.id with no caller-supplied id, so the test would only be a regression guard not a live hole, and (b) the orphan window is narrower than claimed since deleteUser's CASCADE sweeps most rows; the real residual risk is linq_group_chats (SET NULL, not CASCADE). Still a real, currently-present issue worth fixing.</sub>

- **[MED]** Highest-blast-radius route (account/delete) has zero test coverage and a non-atomic, unverified cascade — `src/app/api/account/delete/route.ts:23-41`

(3) Non-atomic confirmed — no RPC/transaction wrapper; five separate statements. Severity is correctly medium, not a blocker: no unauthorized access or data exposure, only incomplete deletion on a rare partial failure against a privacy-removal promise. The finding's own call — that the partial-failure ordering warrants a real test over a model's read — is the right disposition.</sub>

So the promoteError -> 500 branch at route.ts:127-133 has zero coverage, and the happy-path test (route.test.ts:302-333) runs with updateError=null. Cited code/test locations are accurate. However medium overstates it: the code is already correct (it returns 500, not a false {verified:true}, and verified stays false on failure — no security/data-integrity defect), and the finding's retry-hazard sub-claim is wrong because the promote is a single atomic .update() of all five fields, so a failed write leaves the row fully intact (code still valid, fields not cleared) and a retry is safe rather than hitting an inconsistent row.
